# Progress — Garling, *A Course in Galois Theory* (1986)

- **Source text:** D. J. H. Garling, *A Course in Galois Theory* (Cambridge University Press, 1986; ISBN `0 521 32077 1` / `0 521 31249 3`).
- **Source-text provenance:** Staged 1986 CUP scan `garling1986.pdf` (6,924,939 B, MD5 `f0eaa8b28b59e9c8be6d59c05130a703`) and full-text extraction `garling1986_pages.txt` (316,147 B, MD5 `a1c10c3ef4129917c3fcfd9c8ec1fbfd`), authorized as the Stage 1 working copy by Derek on 2026-11-14 (~21:34 PST; `derekblair/formalize-everything#2`).
- **Toolchain & Mathlib pins:** `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`.
- **Novelty gate (FE #2, 2026-11-06, comment `6689821337`):** `PARTIAL` — no book-level formalization of Garling exists in any proof assistant.

## Chapter 1 Scope of Record (Enumerated from 1986 Scan, Printed pp. 3–13 / PDF pp. 11–21)

All definitions in Chapter 1 (§1.1 *Groups*, §1.2 *Fields*, §1.3 *Vector spaces*) and early prose theorems (Lagrange's theorem, first isomorphism theorem, Viergruppe normality in $\Sigma_4$, subgroup classification of $\mathbb{Z}$, $\sqrt{2} \notin \mathbb{Q}$, and subfield $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2}) \subseteq \mathbb{R}$) are unnumbered prose in the 1986 CUP edition (and are also formalized in `Garling/Chapter01.lean` to anchor the chapter's vocabulary). The chapter contains **7 main-text theorem/corollary results** (all in §1.3) and **18 numbered exercises** (`1.1`–`1.18`), listed below in exact source order:

## Chapter 2 Scope of Record (Enumerated from 1986 Scan, Printed pp. 14–17 / PDF pp. 22–25)

All definitions and examples in Chapter 2 (§2.1 *The axiom of choice*, §2.2 *Zorn's lemma*, §2.3 *The existence of a basis*) — namely the Cartesian-product statement of the axiom of choice (§2.1, p. 14), partial order, inclusion order on subsets, totally ordered set, chain (explicitly defined as a *non-empty* totally ordered subset), upper bound, the finite-subsets-of-an-infinite-set example, and maximal element (§2.2, p. 15), well-ordering (introduced inside Exercise 2.2, §2.3, p. 17), and the prose deduction that every vector space has a basis (§2.3, p. 16) — are unnumbered in the 1986 CUP edition (and are formalized in `Garling/Chapter02.lean` to anchor the chapter's vocabulary). The chapter contains **1 numbered main-text theorem** (`Theorem 2.1` in §2.3) plus **1 unnumbered bold-headed lemma block** (`Zorn's lemma` in §2.2, stated without proof in the book) = **2 main-text headed results**, and **4 numbered exercises** (`2.1`–`2.4`, all in §2.3), listed below in exact source order:

|---:|---|---|---:|---|---|---|

## Chapter 3 Scope of Record (Enumerated from 1986 Scan, Printed pp. 18–36 / PDF pp. 26–44; Part 2 Divider Printed pp. 37–38 / PDF pp. 45–46)

All definitions and examples in Chapter 3 (§3.1 *Rings*, §3.2 *Integral domains*, §3.3 *Ideals*, §3.4 *Irreducibles, primes and unique factorization domains*, §3.5 *Principal ideal domains*, §3.6 *Highest common factors*, §3.7 *Polynomials over unique factorization domains*, §3.8 *The existence of maximal proper ideals*, §3.9 *More about fields*) — including commutative ring with $1 \ne 0$, polynomial rings $R[x]$, $R[x_1, \dots, x_n]$, and $R[X_S]$, integral domain, field of fractions construction, ring homomorphisms, ideals, principal ideals, quotient rings $R/J$, characteristic $\operatorname{char} R$, units, irreducibles, $PP$, ACCPI, associates, unique factorization domain, length $l(r)$, prime elements, principal ideal domain, highest common factor, relatively prime subsets, polynomial content and primitive polynomials, maximal proper ideals, subfields, and prime subfield — are unnumbered prose in the 1986 CUP edition (and are formalized in `Garling/Chapter03.lean` to anchor the chapter's vocabulary). The chapter contains **21 main-text theorem/corollary results** (**17 numbered theorems** `Theorem 3.1`–`Theorem 3.17` + **4 corollaries**: `Corollary (Gauss' lemma)` [to Theorem 3.12], `Corollary 1` [to Theorem 3.13], `Corollary 2` [to Theorem 3.13], and `Corollary` [to Theorem 3.16]) and **27 numbered exercises** (`3.1`–`3.27`), listed below in exact source order (**48** items total):

## Explicit Formulation Notes (No Silent Deviations)

1. **Order / cardinality without junk values (`setOrder`):** Garling (§1.1, p. 4) defines the order $|S|$ of a set $S$ to be the number of elements in $S$, taking values in $\mathbb{N} \cup \{+\infty\}$. To avoid `Nat.card` / `Set.ncard`'s junk value `0` on infinite sets, `Garling.Chapter01.setOrder` is defined as `ENat.card S : ℕ∞`, and right cosets `RightCoset H g = {h * g | h ∈ H}` and `RightCosets H` are defined literally as on p. 4 (used in `lagrange_theorem`, `exercise_1_3`, `exercise_1_4`, `theorem_1_2`, `theorem_1_3`, `corollary_to_theorem_1_2`, and `corollary_1_to_theorem_1_4`).
2. **Subset linear independence, basis, and dimension without junk values:** Following Garling (§1.3, p. 10 & p. 12), `IsLinearlyDependent`, `IsLinearlyIndependent`, `IsBasis`, and `IsFiniteDimensional` are defined directly on subsets `A : Set V` (and bridged to Mathlib's `LinearIndepOn K id A` via `isLinearlyIndependent_iff`). `garlingDim K V (hV : IsFiniteDimensional K V) : ℕ` requires an explicit `IsFiniteDimensional K V` proof rather than returning `0` on infinite-dimensional spaces.
3. **Exercise 1.14(c) (`{e_1, f_1, f_2, ..., f_n}` in `K^n`, printed p. 13):** Visually verified in the 150 DPI render of printed p. 13 (`PDF p. 21`), Garling defines $f_j = e_1 + \dots + e_j$ (so $f_1 = e_1$) and asks in part (c): *"Is $\{e_1, f_1, f_2, \dots, f_n\}$ a basis for $K^n$?"* (for $n \ge 1$, typed via `Fin (n + 1)`). Because $e_1 = f_1$:
   - As a **subset** of $K^{n+1}$ (Garling's §1.3 definition of basis), `insert e_1 {f_1, ..., f_{n+1}} = {f_1, ..., f_{n+1}}` **is** a basis (`exercise_1_14_c_set`).
   - As an **indexed `(n + 2)`-tuple** `(e_1, f_1, f_2, ..., f_{n+1})` (`garlingEx114cTuple`), the repeated vector $e_1 = f_1$ makes the family **not** linearly independent (`exercise_1_14_c_tuple_not_linearIndependent`).
   Both statements are kernel-proved and conjoined in `exercise_1_14` so neither reading is left unaddressed.
4. **Exercises 1.7, 1.10, and 1.18:** Held in `QUEUE.md` as fidelity holds with both kernel-proved counterexamples to the literal degenerate/false printed claims and kernel-proved repaired statements.
5. **Axiom of Choice (§2.1, printed p. 14) and non-vacuous Zorn implications (Exercises 2.1–2.2, printed pp. 16–17):** Garling (§2.1, p. 14) states the Axiom of Choice via non-emptiness of the Cartesian product $\prod_{\alpha \in A} E_\alpha$ of non-empty sets $E_\alpha$. `Garling/Chapter02.lean` defines `CartesianProduct E = {c : A → X | ∀ α, c α ∈ E α}`, proves equivalence between the subset-family statement `GarlingAxiomOfChoice` and the dependent-family statement `GarlingAxiomOfChoiceDep` (`garlingAxiomOfChoice_iff_dep`), and proves `axiom_of_choice` / `garlingAxiomOfChoice_holds`. Because Lean's classical foundation already includes `Classical.choice`, `exercise_2_1` (`exercise_2_1_axiomOfChoice`, `exercise_2_1_dep`) and `exercise_2_2` (`exercise_2_2_set`) take Zorn's lemma (`hzorn : GarlingZornLemma`) as an **explicit hypothesis** and construct Garling's exact partial-choice poset `Ex21PartialChoice` and initial-segment well-ordered-subset poset `Ex22WellOrderedSub` rather than proving the conclusions directly from `Classical.choice` ignoring `hzorn`.
6. **Zorn's lemma (§2.2, printed p. 15 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** On p. 15, Garling defines a partial order on an arbitrary set $S$ (not requiring $S \ne \emptyset$), then explicitly defines a chain as *"A non-empty subset $C$ of a partially ordered set $S$..."*, and states Zorn's lemma without requiring $S \ne \emptyset$. For $S = \emptyset$, there are no non-empty subsets $C \subseteq \emptyset$, so every chain vacuously has an upper bound while $S = \emptyset$ has no maximal element (`zorn_lemma_empty_counterexample`). Both the `S.Nonempty` / `Nonempty S` guarded versions (`zorn_lemma_set`, `zorn_lemma`, `garlingZornLemma_holds`, `garlingZornLemma_iff_set`) and Halmos's possibly-empty-chains formulation (`zorn_lemma_possibly_empty_chains`, citing footnote 1 on p. 15) are kernel-proved, and the unnumbered Zorn's lemma block is held in `QUEUE.md`.
7. **Exercise 2.3 (§2.3, printed p. 17 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** In Part 2 (*"...while $\{x : x < d\}$ is countable for each $d < c$"*), taking $d$ to be the least element of $A$ gives $\{x : x < d\} = \emptyset$, confirming that "countable" in Garling includes finite sets (`Set.Countable`, $|S| \le \aleph_0$) and "uncountable" means `¬ Countable` ($> \aleph_0$; matching Ch. 4 pp. 39, 43 & Ch. 18 p. 143). Both parts of Exercise 2.3 are false as printed (`(ℕ, ≤)` refutes Part 1 in `exercise_2_3_infinite_counterexample`, and `Omega1 = (ℵ₁).ord.ToType` refutes Part 2 in `exercise_2_3_uncountable_counterexample`). `Garling/Chapter02.lean` proves both counterexamples, the exact characterizations (`exercise_2_3_infinite_iff` / `_raw`, `exercise_2_3_uncountable_iff` / `_raw`), the dichotomies (`exercise_2_3_infinite_dichotomy`, `exercise_2_3_uncountable_dichotomy`), uniqueness up to order isomorphism of `(ℕ, ≤)` as the infinite counterexample (`exercise_2_3_orderIso_nat_of_forall_Iio_finite`), and the repaired sufficiency theorems (`exercise_2_3_infinite_of_uncountable` and `exercise_2_3_uncountable_of_aleph1_lt`).
8. **Exercise 2.4 (§2.3, printed p. 17):** Both the Mathlib `[LinearOrder] [WellFoundedLT]` formulation (`exercise_2_4`) and the raw `IsGarlingWellOrder` relation formulation (`exercise_2_4_raw`) are proved. `isOrderPreservingBijection_iff` proves that on well-ordered (or linearly ordered) sets, one-way monotonicity (`x₁ ≤ x₂ → i x₁ ≤ i x₂`) and two-way order isomorphism (`x₁ ≤ x₂ ↔ i x₁ ≤ i x₂`) coincide for every bijection, and `ex24Branch2_iff` / `ex24Branch3_iff` prove equivalence between the dependent-pair `∃!` formulation and separate uniqueness of the cut point and the bijection.
9. **Garling rings (`1 ≠ 0`), polynomial degree without junk values (`garlingDegree`), and explicit field of fractions (§3.1–§3.2, printed pp. 18–21):** Garling (§3.1, p. 18) defines every *ring* to be commutative with $1 \ne 0$, formalized as `IsGarlingRing R add mul` and bridged to `[CommRing R] [Nontrivial R]` via `isGarlingRing_iff_nontrivial`. Polynomial degree `garlingDegree a (ha : a ≠ 0) : ℕ` (§3.1, p. 19) takes an explicit `a ≠ 0` proof (with `garlingDegree_spec`) so degree `0` is never conflated with $a = 0$, and §3.2's equivalence-class construction on $R \times R^*$ is formalized directly as `GarlingFieldOfFractions R` and proved ring-isomorphic to Mathlib's `FractionRing R` (`garlingFieldOfFractionsEquiv`).
10. **Literal Chapter 3 factorization and ideal predicates bridged to Mathlib (§3.3–§3.8, printed pp. 21–35):** `IsGarlingIntegralDomain`, `IsGarlingRingHom`, `IsGarlingIdeal`, `IsProperIdeal`, `IsGarlingUnit`, `IsGarlingIrreducible`, `PP` (proper principal ideals), `SatisfiesACCPI`, `IsGarlingAssociates`, `IsGarlingUFD` + `ufdLength`, `IsGarlingPrime`, `IsGarlingPrimeIdeal`, `IsGarlingPID`, `IsGarlingHCF`, `IsGarlingRelativelyPrime`, `IsGarlingLCM`, `IsGarlingContent`, `IsGarlingPrimitive`, `IsGarlingMaximalProperIdeal`, `polyIdeal`, and `primeSubfield` are all defined literally as printed and proved equivalent to their Mathlib counterparts (`isGarlingIntegralDomain_iff`, `isGarlingIdeal_iff`, `isGarlingIrreducible_iff`, `satisfiesACCPI_iff_wfDvdMonoid`, `isGarlingUFD_iff_uniqueFactorizationMonoid`, `isGarlingPrime_iff`, `isGarlingPrimeIdeal_iff`, `isGarlingPID_iff`, `isGarlingPrimitive_iff`, `isGarlingMaximalProperIdeal_iff`, `primeSubfield_eq_bot`).
11. **Exercises 3.1 and 3.11 (`QUEUE.md` fidelity holds):** Exercise 3.1 Part 1 (`R^S` is a ring) fails at $S = \emptyset$ because $1 = 0$ in $R^\emptyset$ (`exercise_3_1_empty_counterexample`), violating p. 18 condition (d) (`1 ≠ 0`); `exercise_3_1_isGarlingRing_iff`, `[Nonempty S]`-guarded `exercise_3_1`, and unconditional `exercise_3_1_zero_divisors` (Part 2) are all kernel-proved. Exercise 3.11 (`a ∈ R` prime iff `R/(a)` is an integral domain) fails at $a = 0$ because p. 26 defines prime elements to be **non-zero** while $R/(0) \cong R$ is an integral domain (`exercise_3_11_zero_counterexample`); both `a ≠ 0`-guarded `exercise_3_11` and the unconditional characterization `exercise_3_11_iff_zero_or_prime` (`IsDomain (R ⧸ (a)) ↔ a = 0 ∨ Prime a`) are kernel-proved.
12. **Exercise 3.17 (§3.5, printed p. 29 — minor notation slip `S` vs. `R` in printed text):** The printed text reads *"Let $R$ be the set of rationals which can be written in the form $r/s$, where $p$ does not divide $s$. Show that $S$ is a subring of $\mathbb{Q}$. What are the units in $R$? Show that $R$ is a principal ideal domain"* (and Exercise 3.27 on p. 36 refers back to *"Let $R$ be the ring of Exercise 3.17"*). Clearly *"Show that $S$ is a subring of $\mathbb{Q}$"* is a typographical slip for $R$; `RatLocalAtPrime p hp : Subring ℚ` formalizes this subring and `exercise_3_17` proves unit characterization, unique $p^k u$ factorization, ideal classification $(p^k)$, and `IsGarlingPID`.
13. **Theorem 3.14 and Exercise 3.24 (§3.8, printed p. 34):** `theorem_3_14` is proved directly from Chapter 2's `zorn_lemma_set` following Garling's chain-union argument on p. 34, while `exercise_3_24` (via `exercise_3_24_of_surjective`) constructs the maximal proper ideal recursively along a surjective enumeration $\mathbb{N} \twoheadrightarrow R$ (`Nat.rec` on `I ⊔ Ideal.span {r n} ≠ ⊤`) without invoking Zorn's lemma.
14. **Exercise 3.27 (§3.9, printed p. 36 — both readings of "quotient fields"):** Placed in §3.9 right after Theorem 3.16 ($R/J$ is a field iff $J$ is maximal) and Theorem 3.17 ($\mathbb{Z}_p$), *"What are the possible quotient fields of $R$?"* asks for the quotient rings $R/J$ of $R = \{r/s \in \mathbb{Q} : p \nmid s\}$ that are fields (namely $J = (p)$ uniquely, with $R/(p) \cong \mathbb{Z}_p$). Because "quotient field" in English algebra can also mean field of fractions (`Garling` calls that *field of fractions* in §3.2), `exercise_3_27` kernel-proves **both**: `∀ J, IsField (R ⧸ J) ↔ J = Ideal.span {(p : R)}`, `Nonempty ((R ⧸ Ideal.span {(p : R)}) ≃+* ZMod p)`, and `Nonempty (FractionRing R ≃+* ℚ)`.
15. **Extension triples `(i, K, L)` vs. subfield `L:K` and degree `extDegree : ℕ∞` without junk values (§4.2, printed pp. 40–42):** Garling (§4.2, p. 40) defines an extension first as a triple $(i, K, L)$ where $i : K \to L$ is a ring monomorphism, then identifies $K$ with the subfield $i(K) \subseteq L$ (`FieldExtensionTriple.imageEquiv`), writing $L:K$. `Garling/Chapter04.lean` formalizes `FieldExtensionTriple K L` and proves both `[Algebra K L]` and explicit triple/subfield forms where relevant (`theorem_4_1`, `theorem_4_1_triple`, `theorem_4_1_subfield`, `exercise_4_1`, `exercise_4_1_subfield`, `theorem_4_5`, `theorem_4_5_triple`, `theorem_4_7`, `theorem_4_7_triple`, `theorem_4_8`, `theorem_4_8_subfield`). Because Mathlib's `Module.finrank K L : ℕ` returns the junk value `0` on infinite extensions, `extDegree K L : ℕ∞` is defined via Chapter 1's `IsFiniteDimensional` and `garlingDim` (`extDegree_eq_garlingDim`, `extDegree_eq_finrank`, `extDegree_eq_setOrder_basis`, `one_le_extDegree`, `extDegree_ne_zero`) so `theorem_4_2` (`extDegree K M = extDegree L M * extDegree K L`), the $n$-step `tower_law`, and `exercise_4_2` (`extDegree K L ≤ extDegree K K₁ * extDegree K K₂`) hold unconditionally across both finite and infinite extensions without `0 * ⊤` pathologies.
16. **Literal Chapter 4 generated-subfield, minimal-polynomial, and algebraic-extension definitions bridged to Mathlib (§4.3–§4.5, printed pp. 42–48):** Garling's intersection definition $K(A)$ (`garlingFieldGenSubfield`) and finite-product ratio construction $\{rs^{-1} : r \in V, s \in V^*\}$ (`garlingFiniteProducts`, `garlingGenSubspace`, `garlingFieldGen_eq_ratios`), `IsSimpleExtension`, `IsGarlingAlgebraic`, `IsGarlingTranscendental`, `evalRingHom` ($E_\alpha$), `evalKer` ($K_\alpha$), `exists_unique_monic_generator_evalKer` ($m_\alpha$), `algebraicElementsSet` / `algebraicSubfield` ($L_a$), `IsAlgebraicExtension`, `GarlingAlgebraicNumbers` ($A \subseteq \mathbb{C}$), `IsFinitelyGeneratedExtension`, `FixesBaseField`, and `IsFieldAutomorphism` are all defined literally as printed and proved equivalent to their Mathlib counterparts (`garlingFieldGenSubfield_eq_adjoin`, `isSimpleExtension_iff_garling`, `isGarlingAlgebraic_iff`, `isGarlingAlgebraic_iff_isIntegral`, `isAlgebraicExtension_iff`, `isFinitelyGeneratedExtension_iff_garling`). In `Theorem 4.6` (p. 46), equivalence `(i) ↔ (ii) ↔ (iii)` is proved both for `Fin n → L` (`n ≥ 0`, `theorem_4_6`) and for non-empty `Fin (n + 1) → L` (`n ≥ 1`, `theorem_4_6_nonempty_tuple`, since $1 \in K$ is always algebraic over $K$). In `Corollary 2` [to Theorem 4.6] (p. 47, printed $S \subset L$), `corollary_2_to_theorem_4_6` is proved for every subset `S : Set L` (`S ⊆ L`), covering both strict and inclusive subset readings.
17. **Exercise 4.3 (§4.3, printed p. 45 — `det(xI - T_α) = m_α`):** Because Chapter 4 uses $\det(xI - T_\alpha)$ for the linear map $T_\alpha(\beta) = \alpha\beta$ on the finite simple extension $K(\alpha)$ over $K$, `exercise_4_3` proves the exact identity with `minpoly K α` in **all three** standard formalizations simultaneously: (i) `LinearMap.charpoly (mulByGenLinearMap K α) = minpoly K α`, (ii) the basis-free $K[X]$-module base-change determinant `LinearMap.det (Polynomial.X • id - baseChange K[X] (mulByGenLinearMap K α)) = minpoly K α`, and (iii) for every finite indexing type `ι` and every $K$-basis `b : Module.Basis ι K K(α)`, the matrix determinant `(Polynomial.X • 1 - (LinearMap.toMatrix b b (mulByGenLinearMap K α)).map Polynomial.C).det = minpoly K α`.
18. **Exercise 4.5 (§4.3, printed p. 45 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** Held in `QUEUE.md` as a source-fidelity hold because the literal printed hypothesis that $[L(\alpha):L]$ and $[L:K]$ are relatively prime is mathematically false (Garling printed $[L(\alpha):L]$ in place of $[K(\alpha):K]$, and coprimality also requires the two degrees to be finite natural numbers rather than $\infty$). `Garling/Chapter04.lean` kernel-proves the non-degenerate counterexample $K = \mathbb{Q}, L = \mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2}), \alpha = 2^{1/6}$ (`[L(α):L] = 3`, `[L:K] = 2`, `minpoly L α = X^3 - C √2 ∉ ℚ[X]`, `exercise_4_5_printed_counterexample`), the degenerate $\alpha = \sqrt{2}$ counterexample (`exercise_4_5_degenerate_counterexample`), the exact necessary-and-sufficient characterization (`exercise_4_5_coeffs_in_base_iff`), and the `[K(α):K]`-coprime repaired theorems (`exercise_4_5` and `exercise_4_5_garling_relPrime`, bridged via `natCoprime_iff_isGarlingRelativelyPrime`).
19. **Non-`"Show that P"` exercises in Chapter 4 (`Exercises 4.1, 4.9, 4.11`):**
   - `Exercise 4.1` (§4.2, p. 42, *"What fields are there intermediate between $L$ and $K$?"* when $[L:K]$ is prime): proved as `(⊥ : IntermediateField K L) ≠ ⊤ ∧ ∀ M : IntermediateField K L, M = ⊥ ∨ M = ⊤` (`exercise_4_1`) and in subfield form `K ≠ ⊤ ∧ ∀ M : Subfield L, K ≤ M → M = K ∨ M = ⊤` (`exercise_4_1_subfield`).
   - `Exercise 4.9` (§4.4, p. 48, *"Suppose that $a$ and $b$ are complex numbers which are transcendental over $\mathbb{Q}$. Is $a^b$ transcendental over $\mathbb{Q}$?"*): answered completely without branch ambiguity by constructing positive real transcendental witnesses $a > 1, b > 0$ (embedded in $\mathbb{C}$, where `Complex.ofReal_cpow` guarantees `Complex.cpow` and `Real.rpow` coincide canonically) for **both** outcomes: `exercise_4_9_exists_algebraic_power` proves $(a : \mathbb{C})^{(b : \mathbb{C})} = 2 \in \mathbb{Q}$ (so $a^b$ is **not** necessarily transcendental over $\mathbb{Q}$), `exercise_4_9_exists_transcendental_power` proves witnesses $a > 1, b > 1$ for which $(a : \mathbb{C})^{(b : \mathbb{C})}$ **is** transcendental over $\mathbb{Q}$, and `exercise_4_9` packages the explicit refutation of universal transcendence alongside both witness families.
   - `Exercise 4.11` (§4.5, p. 48, *"Show that the condition that $L:K$ is algebraic cannot be dropped from Theorem 4.8"*): `exercise_4_11_for_every_field` proves for **every** field $K$ that $L = \operatorname{FractionRing}(K[X])$ is non-algebraic over $K$ and the $K$-fixing monomorphism induced by $X \mapsto X^2$ is not surjective ($X \notin \operatorname{range} \tau$), and `exercise_4_11` packages both the existential counterexample and the explicit negation of the `IsAlgebraicExtension`-dropped statement of `Theorem 4.8`.
20. **Kronecker factorization algorithms and complexity model (`Theorem 5.1` and `Exercises 5.1–5.3`, §5.1, printed pp. 49–50 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity holds):** Unlike §3.4 Theorem 3.4 (*"every non-zero element of $R$ which is not a unit"*), `Theorem 5.1` and `Exercise 5.3` quantify over *"any element"* of $\mathbb{Z}[x]$ (resp. $K[x]$ for finite $K$), which fails on $0$ and units (`theorem_5_1_zero_counterexample`, `theorem_5_1_unit_counterexample`, `exercise_5_3_zero_counterexample`, `exercise_5_3_unit_counterexample`), and both rely on p. 49's informal prose description of *algorithm*, while `Exercise 5.1` asks for a computer-programme outline and `Exercise 5.2` asks for a calculation upper bound without a formal cost model. `Garling/Chapter05.lean` constructs **total computable** `List ℤ`-level Lean procedures (`kroneckerProgramme`, `kroneckerIsIrreducibleWithSteps`, `kroneckerCalcBound`) together with `noncomputable def` `Polynomial`-typed wrappers (`kroneckerFactorWithUnit`, `kroneckerFactorList`, `finiteFieldFactorWithUnit`, `finiteFieldFactorList`) via exact divisor-tuple enumeration and Lagrange interpolation (`kroneckerInterpPoly_spec`, `lagrangeInterpCoeffs?_complete`), proves complete soundness/completeness theorems (`exercise_5_1`, `theorem_5_1`, `theorem_5_1_iff`, `exercise_5_2`, `exercise_5_3`, `exercise_5_3_finite`, `exercise_5_3_iff`), and holds all four items in `QUEUE.md` pending coordinator ruling.
21. **Linear pencil `f - y g ∈ K(y)[x]` (`Exercise 5.4`, §5.1, printed p. 50 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** In `Exercise 5.4`, $y$ is an implicit second indeterminate over $K$, formalized canonically via `K(y) = FractionRing K[X]` with `y = ratFuncVar K = algebraMap K[X] (FractionRing K[X]) X` and `linearPencilRatFunc f g = f.map (algebraMap K K(y)) - C y * g.map (algebraMap K K(y))`. When both $f, g \in K[X]$ are constants ($\max(\deg f, \deg g) = 0$), $\{f, g\}$ can still be relatively prime under §3.6 `IsGarlingRelativelyPrime` (and `IsRelPrime`), e.g. $(1, 1)$, $(1, 0)$, and $(0, 1)$, where $f - yg$ equals $1 - y$, $1$, and $-y$ — all units in $K(y)[x]$ and not irreducible (`exercise_5_4_constant_counterexample`). `exercise_5_4` (and `exercise_5_4_relPrime`) proves irreducibility whenever `0 < f.natDegree ∨ 0 < g.natDegree`, and `exercise_5_4_iff` proves that for relatively prime $f, g \in K[X]$, `Irreducible (linearPencilRatFunc f g) ↔ 0 < f.natDegree ∨ 0 < g.natDegree`.
22. **Degree of `K(α):K(β)` for transcendental `α` (`Exercise 5.5`, §5.1, printed p. 51):** Carrying Sentence 2's hypothesis $\beta \notin K$ (`β ∉ (⊥ : IntermediateField K L)`) into Sentence 3 (`β = f(α)/g(α)` with `{f, g}` relatively prime in `K[X]`), `exercise_5_5` deduces `f ≠ 0`, `g ≠ 0`, and `g(α) ≠ 0` automatically from `β ∉ K` (since `0 / x = 0 ∈ K` and `x / 0 = 0 ∈ K` in Lean fields) without adding any unprinted non-vanishing hypothesis, and proves `IsFiniteExtension K(β) K(α)`, `IsGarlingAlgebraic K(β) α`, `IsGarlingTranscendental K β`, and `extDegree K(β) K(α) = max (garlingDegree f hf0) (garlingDegree g hg0) = max f.natDegree g.natDegree`. (Note: in Chapter 18, Exercise 18.1 on p. 141 and Lüroth's proof on p. 145 mis-cite `Exercise 5.5` as `Exercise 5.3`.) For completeness, `exercise_5_5_standalone_constant_counterexample` also records that reading Sentence 3 in isolation without `β ∉ K` fails at `f = g = 1`.
23. **Eisenstein's criterion over arbitrary integral domains vs. Localization (`Theorem 5.2`, `Exercises 5.6–5.7`, and `Theorem 5.3`, §§5.2–5.3, printed pp. 51–53):** `Theorem 5.2` and `Exercise 5.6` are formalized in Garling's full generality for **arbitrary integral domains** `[CommRing R] [IsDomain R]` (not restricted to UFDs) using Chapter 3's `IsGarlingRelativelyPrime` and `IsGarlingPrime` / `Prime`. Because `Theorem 5.2`, `Exercise 5.6`, and `Exercise 5.7` omit $n \ge 1$ and fail at $n = 0$ (`theorem_5_2_zero_counterexample`, `exercise_5_6_zero_counterexample`, `exercise_5_7_zero_counterexample`), all three are held in `QUEUE.md` alongside their `1 ≤ n` proofs and exact `Irreducible ↔ 1 ≤ n` characterizations (`theorem_5_2_iff_pos`, `exercise_5_6_iff_pos`, `exercise_5_7_iff`). By contrast, `Theorem 5.3 (Localization principle)` (p. 52) holds **literally for all `n : ℕ`** (`theorem_5_3`, `theorem_5_3_garling`) because at $n = 0$, $\bar{f} \in \mathbb{Z}_p[x]$ is constant over the field $\mathbb{Z}_p$ (`ZMod p`) and hence never irreducible. All four main-text worked examples (`example_5_1_cubic_irreducible`, `example_5_2_quintic_irreducible`, `example_5_3_quartic_shift_irreducible`, and `example_5_3_trinomial_irreducible` for all `n : ℕ`) are also kernel-proved.
24. **Shorthand *"irreducible over `F`"* (`Exercise 5.10`, p. 52; `Exercise 5.11`, p. 53):** Matching p. 51's main-text usage (*"irreducible over $\mathbb{Z}[x]$ ... irreducible over $\mathbb{Q}[x]$"*), *"irreducible over $\mathbb{Q}(i)$"* in `Exercise 5.10` is formalized as irreducibility in `gaussianRationals[X]` (`gaussianRationals = IntermediateField.adjoin ℚ {Complex.I} : IntermediateField ℚ ℂ`), and *"irreducible over $\mathbb{Q}$"* in `Exercise 5.11` is formalized as irreducibility of `garlingPrimeCyclotomicRat p` in `ℚ[X]` (proved via `polyShiftEquiv ℤ 1`, matching the printed substitution $y = x - 1 \iff x = y + 1$, in `exercise_5_11_int` + `corollary_gauss_lemma`).
25. **Minimal-polynomial identification questions (`Exercise 5.12`, §5.3, printed p. 53):** Following the direct-identification convention of `Exercise 3.27` and `Exercise 4.1`, the two *"What is the minimal polynomial...?"* questions for $\theta = 2\pi/7$ (`2 * Real.pi / 7`) are proved directly as `minpoly ℚ (Complex.exp (Complex.I * (θ : ℂ))) = X^6 + X^5 + X^4 + X^3 + X^2 + X + 1` (`exercise_5_12_exp`) and `minpoly ℚ (2 * Real.cos θ) = X^3 + X^2 - 2 * X - 1` (`exercise_5_12_two_cos`), together with `evalKer ℚ` ideal-generator identifications and conjoined in `exercise_5_12`.
26. **Constructible points, fixed base points `P_0 = (0, 0)` and `P_1 = (1, 0)`, and equivalence of `n ≥ 1` / positive-radius vs. `n ≥ 0` / zero-radius conventions (`§6.1`, printed pp. 54–56):** Garling (§6.1, pp. 54–55) fixes the origin $P_0 = (0, 0)$ and unit first-axis point $P_1 = (1, 0)$, writes $S_j = \{P_0, P_1, \dots, P_j\}$ for $1 \le j \le n$, and requires each $P_j$ ($2 \le j \le n$) to arise via `(i)` two distinct straight lines joining distinct points of $S_{j-1}$ (`inter_eq_singleton_of_distinct_lines` proves $L_1 \cap L_2 = \{P\}$), `(ii)` a straight line and a circle, or `(iii)` two distinct circles (`distinct_centres_of_distinct_intersecting_circles` proves p. 55's remark that intersecting distinct circles must have distinct centres). `Garling/Chapter06.lean` formalizes both `IsConstructible` ($n \ge 1$, distinct line-defining points $A \ne B$, positive-radius circles $R_1 \ne R_2$; `P_1` constructible at $n = 1$ in `isConstructible_P₁`, `P_0` constructible at $n = 3$ via `(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2)` and `(1/2, -\sqrt{3}/2)` in `isConstructible_P₀`) and `IsConstructibleRelaxed` ($n \ge 0$, allowing zero-radius circles $R_1 = R_2$), and proves their exact equivalence `isConstructible_iff_relaxed`. `theorem_6_1` and `theorem_6_1_subfield` prove `IsFiniteExtension ℚ ℚ(x, y)` and `∃ r : ℕ, extDegree ℚ ℚ(x, y) = 2 ^ r` in both `IntermediateField ℚ ℝ` (`ratPointField x y`) and Chapter 4's `garlingFieldGenSubfield ℚ ({x, y} : Set ℝ)` form.
27. **Exercise 6.1 (§6.1, printed p. 57 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** Like `Exercises 5.1–5.2`, `Exercise 6.1` (*"Describe how the constructions mentioned in the first paragraph of Section 6.1 are made"*) is a non-propositional expository prompt, and stating its geometric operations (equilateral triangle/square/regular hexagon, angle bisection, perpendicular from a point to a line, parallel line through a point, rational-ratio interval division, plus rational points) as closure theorems requires explicit parameterizations and non-degeneracy hypotheses (`A ≠ B` for lines, `A ≠ O ∧ B ≠ O` for angle rays, `p + q ≠ 0` for homogeneous rational ratio `p : q`; when `A = B`, `exercise_6_1_degenerate_line_counterexample` proves `garlingLine A A = Set.univ`). `Garling/Chapter06.lean` kernel-proves all six construction families (`exercise_6_1_polygons`, `exercise_6_1_bisect_angle`, `exercise_6_1_drop_perpendicular`, `exercise_6_1_parallel_line`, `exercise_6_1_divide_interval_rat`, `exercise_6_1_rational_point`, bundled in `exercise_6_1`) via the subfield `constructibleSubfield : Subfield ℝ` (`isConstructible_iff_coords`) and `Exercise 6.2`, and holds `Exercise 6.1` in `QUEUE.md` pending coordinator ruling.
28. **Regular `n`-gon constructibility formulations (`Exercises 6.3` & `6.6`, §§6.1–6.2, printed p. 57; cf. §19.3, printed p. 152):** Chapter 6 defines *constructible point* rather than *constructible polygon*, while §19.3 (printed p. 152) defines constructing a regular $n$-gon ($n > 2$) as constructing a regular $n$-gon of unit side length and equates it with constructing $(\cos(2\pi/n), \sin(2\pi/n))$. For every $n \ge 3$, `regularPolygon_constructible_iff` proves equivalence of all four natural formulations: `(A)` `IsGarlingUnitCircleGeneratorConstructible n` ($(\cos(2\pi/n), \sin(2\pi/n))$), `(B)` `IsUnitCircleRegularPolygonConstructible n` (all $n$ unit-circle vertices), `(C)` `IsUnitSideRegularPolygonConstructible n` (all $n$ vertices of the unit-side regular $n$-gon built on $P_0 P_1$, `unitSideRegularPolygonVertex`), and `(D)` `IsAnyRegularPolygonConstructible n` (existence of *any* non-degenerate Euclidean regular $n$-gon in $\mathbb{R}^2$ with centre $O$, circumradius $R > 0$, phase $\varphi \in \mathbb{R}$, either orientation, and constructible vertices). `exercise_6_3` proves all four for $n = 5$ (via `cos_two_pi_div_five : cos(2π/5) = (√5 - 1)/4`), and `exercise_6_6` (`exercise_6_6_a`, `exercise_6_6_b`) refutes all four for $n = 9$ and $n = 7$ (using Chapter 5's `example_5_1_cubic_irreducible` and `exercise_5_12_two_cos`).
29. **§6.2 Unnumbered prose trisection proof and `Exercise 6.5` (§6.2, printed p. 57):** Although §6.2 (*The angle $\pi/3$ cannot be trisected*) carries no `Theorem 6.2` number (so the strictly numbered item count of Chapter 6 remains `1 + 7 = 8`), its 19-line prose body gives the complete impossibility proof. `angle_pi_div_three_cannot_be_trisected` (and `not_isConstructible_cos_sin_pi_div_nine`) kernel-proves that $(\cos(\pi/3), \sin(\pi/3))$ is constructible while $(\cos(\pi/9), \sin(\pi/9))$ is not and no constructible point $Q \ne P_0$ lies on the trisecting line $x \sin(\pi/9) = y \cos(\pi/9)$ (explicitly invoking `theorem_4_4`, `theorem_4_2`, `theorem_6_1`, and Chapter 5's `example_5_1_cubic_irreducible`). Similarly, `exercise_6_5` proves `¬ IsConstructible ((2 : ℝ) ^ ((1 : ℝ) / 3), 0)` (via Chapter 5's `real_rpow_inv_nat_minpoly_of_prime`) together with the geometric duplication-of-the-cube consequence that no constructible segment $AB$ satisfies `euclidDist A B ^ 3 = 2`.
30. **Quadratic extensions and quadratic towers in `ℝ` (`Exercise 6.7`, §6.3, printed p. 58):** Visually verified on `ch6-066.png`, `Exercise 6.7(a)` assumes $[\mathbb{Q}(x, y) : \mathbb{Q}] = 2$ (`x, y : ℝ`) and `Exercise 6.7(b)` assumes an increasing tower $\mathbb{Q} = F_0 \subseteq F_1 \subseteq \dots \subseteq F_r = \mathbb{Q}(x, y)$ with $[F_{j+1} : F_j] = 2$ for $0 \le j < r$ (including $r = 0$, where $\mathbb{Q}(x, y) = \mathbb{Q}$). Both parts are proved literally in `IntermediateField ℚ ℝ` form (`exercise_6_7_a`, `exercise_6_7_b`, `exercise_6_7`), in `Subfield ℝ` / `garlingFieldGenSubfield` form (`exercise_6_7_a_subfield`, `exercise_6_7_b_subfield`), and in generalized arbitrary-subfield form (`exercise_6_7_a_general`, `exercise_6_7_b_general`).
31. **Literal §7.1 splitting predicates, subfield-minimality splitting-field definition, and zero/constant polynomial edge cases (`Theorem 7.1` & `Theorem 7.3` `QUEUE.md` fidelity holds; printed pp. 60–62):** Garling (§7.1, p. 60) says $f \in K[x]$ *splits over $L$* if $f = \lambda(x - \alpha_1)\dots(x - \alpha_n)$ for $\lambda \in K, \alpha_j \in L$ (including $n = 0$, where the empty product is $1$ so non-zero constants split over $K$, and $\lambda = 0$, so $f = 0$ splits over every extension), and defines $L:K$ to be a *splitting field extension* for $f$ if $f$ splits over $L$ and over no proper intermediate subfield $K \subseteq M \subsetneq L$ (`GarlingSplitsExplicit`, `GarlingSplitsOver`, `IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtension`, proved equivalent to the `Subfield L` formulation via `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtension_iff_subfield`). For $f \ne 0$, `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtension_iff` proves equivalence with Mathlib's `Polynomial.IsSplittingField K L f`, whereas at $f = 0$, `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtension_zero_iff` proves `IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtension K L 0 ↔ (⊥ : IntermediateField K L) = ⊤` ($L = K$). Consequently:
   - `Theorem 7.1` (p. 60) fails literally at $f = 0$: `0 = 0 \cdot (x - i)` splits over $\mathbb{C}$ with $\alpha_1 = i$, yet $\mathbb{R}(i) = \mathbb{C}$ is **not** a splitting field for $0$ over $\mathbb{R}$ because $0$ already splits over $\mathbb{R} \subsetneq \mathbb{C}$ (`theorem_7_1_zero_counterexample`). `theorem_7_1` ($f \ne 0$), `theorem_7_1_nonzero_coeff` ($\lambda \ne 0$), and the exact biconditional `theorem_7_1_iff` (`IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtension K K(α₁,...,αₙ) f ↔ f ≠ 0 ∨ K(α₁,...,αₙ) = ⊥`) are all kernel-proved, and `Theorem 7.1` is held in `QUEUE.md`.
   - `Corollary` [to Theorem 7.1] (p. 60, `corollary_to_theorem_7_1`), `Theorem 7.5` (pp. 64–65, `theorem_7_5`), and `Corollary 1` [to Theorem 7.5] (p. 66, `corollary_1_to_theorem_7_5`, `corollary_1_to_theorem_7_5_equiv`) hold **literally for all `f ∈ K[X]`, including `f = 0` and constants `n = 0`**, with no extra hypotheses.
   - `Theorem 7.3` (p. 61) states `[L:K] ≤ n!` without binding $n$ in its statement (its proof sets $n = \operatorname{degree} f$, which §3.1 defines only for $f \ne 0$). `theorem_7_3_unbound_n_counterexample` refutes universal quantification over unbound $n$ ($n = 0$ on $x^2 + 1 \in \mathbb{R}[x]$ gives $[L:\mathbb{R}] \ge 2 > 0! = 1$), `theorem_7_3` proves existence with `extDegree K L ≤ n!` and `Module.finrank K L ≤ n!` for `n = garlingDegree f hf`, and `theorem_7_3_natDegree` proves existence unconditionally for all `f : K[X]` (including `f = 0`, where $L = K$ has $[K:K] = 1 \le 0! = 1$); `Theorem 7.3` is held in `QUEUE.md`.
32. **Analytic input to `Exercise 7.1` (p. 59) and quotient construction in `Theorem 7.2` (p. 60):** Placed at the end of the p. 59 chapter preamble right after Garling states the Fundamental Theorem of Algebra in $\mathbb{C}[x]$ (`preamble_complex_linear_factorization`), `exercise_7_1` and `exercise_7_1_garling` prove `f.natDegree ≤ 2` / `garlingDegree f f.ne_zero ≤ 2` for every irreducible $f \in \mathbb{R}[x]$ using `Complex.isAlgClosed` and conjugation (prior to Chapter 11 `Exercise 11.11`'s Galois-theoretic proof of FTA). `theorem_7_2`, `theorem_7_2_garling`, and `theorem_7_2_quotient` construct $K(\alpha) = K[x]/(f)$ explicitly from Chapter 3's `Theorem 3.10` / `Theorem 3.16` quotient mechanism and prove simplicity, algebraicity, $[K(\alpha):K] = n$, and $f(\alpha) = 0$.
33. **Induced polynomial map `garlingMapPoly i`, extension of monomorphisms, and `Corollary 1` [to Theorem 7.4] printed typo (`§7.2`, printed pp. 62–66 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** Following p. 63, `garlingMapPoly i : K[X] →+* L[X]` (`Polynomial.mapRingHom i`) extends any ring monomorphism $i : K \to+* L$ coefficientwise (`garlingMapPoly_apply`, `garlingMapPoly_monomorphism`, `garlingSplitsOver_mapPoly_iff`) and `j.comp (algebraMap K M) = i` expresses $j|_K = i$. `theorem_7_4` (arbitrary simple algebraic extension `IsSimpleExtension K Kα` with chosen generator $\alpha$) and `theorem_7_4_adjoin` ($K(\alpha) \subseteq M$), `monomorphismEquivRoot` + `corollary_2_to_theorem_7_4` + `corollary_2_to_theorem_7_4_finset`, `theorem_7_5`, `corollary_1_to_theorem_7_5` + `corollary_1_to_theorem_7_5_equiv` + `splitting_field_unique_up_to_algEquiv`, and `corollary_2_to_theorem_7_5` + `corollary_2_to_theorem_7_5_algEquiv` + `corollary_2_to_theorem_7_5_garling` are all proved literally. On p. 64, `Corollary 1` [to Theorem 7.4] prints *"$\alpha$ is algebraic over $K$, $\alpha'$ algebraic over $K$"* (instead of *"over $K'$"*): `corollary_1_to_theorem_7_4_ambient_counterexample` refutes reading *"$\alpha'$ algebraic over $K$"* in a common ambient extension ($K = \mathbb{Z}_2 \subsetneq K' = \mathbb{Z}_2(t)$ inside $E = \mathbb{Z}_2(s)$ with $t = s^2$, $\alpha' = s$ algebraic over $K'$ with $m_{\alpha', K'} = x^2 - t$ yet transcendental over $K$, and an abstract isomorphism $i : K_0 \cong K'$), while `corollary_1_to_theorem_7_4` and `corollary_1_to_theorem_7_4_equiv` prove the intended `IsGarlingAlgebraic K' α'` statement; `Corollary 1` [to Theorem 7.4] is held in `QUEUE.md`.
34. **Interrogative yes/no exercises answered in the negative (`Exercise 7.4`, p. 67; `Exercise 7.5`, p. 70) and factorial divisibility (`Exercise 7.9`, p. 70):**
   - `Exercise 7.4` (p. 67): $f = x^4 - 2x^3 + 7x^2 - 6x + 12 = (x^2 + 3)(x^2 - 2x + 4)$ is reducible over $\mathbb{Q}$. `exercise_7_4` proves both root claims, proves $\mathbb{Q}(i\sqrt{3})$ is a splitting field for $f$ over $\mathbb{Q}$, proves there is **no** automorphism of $\mathbb{Q}(i\sqrt{3})$ (nor any $\mathbb{Q}$-fixing monomorphism out of *any* extension $L:\mathbb{Q}$ containing $i\sqrt{3}$ and $1 + i\sqrt{3}$) sending $i\sqrt{3} \mapsto 1 + i\sqrt{3}$, and refutes the universal affirmative statement.
   - `Exercise 7.5` (p. 70): `exercise_7_5` refutes universal divisibility $[L(\alpha):L] \mid [K(\alpha):K]$ (both in `Module.finrank` and `extDegree` form) via $K = \mathbb{Q}$, $L = \mathbb{Q}(\omega 2^{1/3})$, $M = \mathbb{C}$, $\alpha = 2^{1/3}$, where $[L(\alpha):L] = 2 \nmid 3 = [K(\alpha):K]$.
   - `Exercise 7.9` (p. 70): `exercise_7_9` and `exercise_7_9_natDegree` prove $[L:K] \mid n!$ (via `splittingField_finrank_dvd_factorial`) for every $n \ge 0$.
35. **§7.3 Worked `Examples 1–4` (printed pp. 67–70):** Formalized as supporting declarations in `Garling/Chapter07.lean`: `example_7_3_1` proves for every prime $p$ and primitive $p$-th root of unity $\omega \in \mathbb{C}$ (instantiated canonically by `primRootOfUnityComplex p` via `primRootOfUnityComplex_spec`) that $x^p - 2 \in \mathbb{Q}[x]$ is irreducible, $\mathbb{Q}(\omega)$ is a splitting field for $x^p - 1$ of degree $p - 1$, $\mathbb{Q}(\omega, 2^{1/p})$ is a splitting field for $x^p - 2$ of degree $p(p - 1)$, and $x^p - 2$ remains irreducible over $\mathbb{Q}(\omega)$ (recalled in Ch. 20 after Theorem 20.3 on p. 160); `example_7_3_2` ($x^6 - 1$, splitting field $\mathbb{Q}(\omega)$ of degree $2$), `example_7_3_3` ($x^6 + 1$, $\omega \notin \mathbb{Q}(i)$, splitting field $\mathbb{Q}(i, \omega)$ of degree $4$), and `example_7_3_4_char_ne_two`, `example_7_3_4_complex_over_real`, `example_7_3_4_zmod2` ($x^2 + ax + b$ for $\operatorname{char} K \ne 2$ and $\mathbb{F}_4 = \mathbb{Z}_2[x]/(x^2 + x + 1)$ over $\mathbb{Z}_2$, not generated by any square root over $\mathbb{Z}_2$) complete §7.3.
36. **Constructive / *"Find / Write down / Construct"* exercises in Chapter 7 (`Exercises 7.2, 7.3, 7.6, 7.7, 7.8, 7.10` — `QUEUE.md` fidelity holds):** Held in `QUEUE.md` per the non-propositional construction-prompt standard (and for `Exercise 7.3`, also because the printed statement omits $n \ge 1$ and fails at $n = 0$, `exercise_7_3_zero_counterexample`), with every mathematical claim and explicit witness kernel-proved in `Garling/Chapter07.lean`:
