   - `Exercise 7.6` (p. 70): `ex76Cubic` + `ex76Cubic_injective` + `ex76Cubic_complete` (all $8$ monic cubics in $\mathbb{Z}_2[x]$), `ex76Cubic_factorizations`, splitting fields `Ex76Field i` of degrees `ex76Deg = ![1,1,1,1,2,2,3,3]`, explicit isomorphism `ex76CubicFieldEquiv : ℤ₂[x]/(x^3+x+1) ≃ₐ[ℤ₂] ℤ₂[x]/(x^3+x^2+1)`, and complete isomorphism classification `Nonempty (Ex76Field i ≃+* Ex76Field j) ↔ Nonempty (Ex76Field i ≃ₐ[ZMod 2] Ex76Field j) ↔ ex76Deg i = ex76Deg j` (`exercise_7_6`).
   - `Exercises 7.7 & 7.8` (p. 70): `exercise_7_7` proves splitting fields, degrees $4, 4, 8$, and primitive elements $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2} + \sqrt{3})$, $\mathbb{Q}(i\sqrt{2} + i\sqrt{3})$, $\mathbb{Q}(5^{1/4} + i)$ for $x^4 - 5x^2 + 6$, $x^4 + 5x^2 + 6$, $x^4 - 5$; `exercise_7_8` proves splitting fields $\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_8), \mathbb{Q}(i), \mathbb{Q}(\zeta_8), \mathbb{Q}(i)$, degrees $4, 2, 4, 2$, and primitive elements $\zeta_8 = (1+i)/\sqrt{2}$ and $i$ for $x^4 + 1$, $x^4 + 4$, $(x^4 + 1)(x^4 + 4)$, $(x^4 - 1)(x^4 + 4)$.
   - `Exercise 7.10` (p. 70): `exercise_7_10` constructs splitting fields for $x^3 - 5$ over $\mathbb{Z}_7$ ($\mathbb{F}_{343} = \mathbb{Z}_7[x]/(x^3 - 5)$, degree $3$), $\mathbb{Z}_{11}$ ($\mathbb{F}_{121} = \mathbb{Z}_{11}[x]/(x^2 + 3x + 9)$, degree $2$), and $\mathbb{Z}_{13}$ ($\mathbb{Z}_{13}$ itself via $(x - 7)(x - 8)(x - 11)$, degree $1$).
37. **Literal cross-chapter splitting, normality, separability, and Galois predicates for Chapter 13 (Chapters 7, 9, 10, 11):** Because Chapters 7–12 have not yet landed on `main`, `Garling/Chapter13.lean` defines Garling's exact cross-chapter vocabulary literally as printed and proves equivalence to Mathlib: `SplitsOver` and `IsGarlingSplittingField` (§7.1, p. 60; `splitsOver_iff_splits`, `isGarlingSplittingField_iff`), `IsGarlingNormalExtension` (§9.1, p. 78; `isGarlingNormalExtension_iff`), `IsGarlingSeparableIrreducible`, `IsGarlingSeparablePoly`, `IsGarlingSeparableElement`, and `IsGarlingSeparableExtension` (§10.1, p. 82; `isGarlingSeparableIrreducible_iff_separable`, `isGarlingSeparablePoly_iff`, `isGarlingSeparableElement_iff`, `isGarlingSeparableExtension_iff`), `IsGarlingGaloisExtension` (§10.3, p. 84; `isGarlingGaloisExtension_iff`), and `GarlingGaloisGroup`, `GarlingPolyGaloisGroup`, and `garlingFixedField` (§11.1, p. 91; `garlingFixedField_coe_subgroup`). Two subtle divergence points between Garling and Mathlib are explicitly witnessed:
   - **Galois extensions include finiteness (§10.3, p. 84):** Garling defines a *Galois extension* as **finite**, normal, and separable (`IsGarlingGaloisExtension K L ↔ FiniteDimensional K L ∧ IsGalois K L`), whereas Mathlib's `IsGalois` omits finiteness. `infinite_isGalois_not_splittingField` proves on `AlgebraicClosure ℚ / ℚ` that `Corollary` [to `Theorem 13.2`] would be **false** under unguarded `[IsGalois K L]`.
   - **Separable reducible polynomials (§10.1, p. 82):** Garling defines a general polynomial $f \in K[x]$ to be *separable* (`IsGarlingSeparablePoly f`) if each of its irreducible factors is separable, permitting repeated separable factors (such as $g = m_{\alpha_1} \cdots m_{\alpha_n}$ in the p. 108 proof of `Theorem 13.2`), whereas Mathlib's `Polynomial.Separable` requires squarefreeness (`mathlib_separable_iff_squarefree_and_isGarlingSeparablePoly`, witnessed on `(X : ℚ[X]) ^ 2` by `x_sq_garlingSeparable_not_mathlibSeparable`).
38. **Intermediate-field criterion, `Exercise 13.1`, and `Exercise 13.2` (§13.1, printed pp. 107–108):** `theorem_13_1` (and `theorem_13_1_algebraic`, `theorem_13_1_subfields`) handles the degenerate $r = 0$ ($L = K$) base case explicitly (`isSimpleExtension_of_bot_eq_top`, `isSimpleExtension_self`, since $K(0) = K$) alongside Garling's p. 107 linear-combination argument (`adjoin_pair_eq_linear_comb_of_finite_intermediateField`) and p. 108 minimal-polynomial-coefficient argument (`intermediateField_eq_adjoin_minpoly_coeffs`). In `Exercise 13.1` (p. 108), `exercise_13_1` deduces `IsSimpleExtension K L` directly from `Garling.Chapter01.exercise_1_17` applied to $L = \bigcup_{\beta \in L} K(\beta)$ over infinite $K$ with `[Finite (IntermediateField K L)]`, requiring **no** algebraicity hypothesis. In `Exercise 13.2` (p. 108), `IsSimpleTranscendentalExtension K L` formalizes p. 43's prose definition, and `exercise_13_2` (`exercise_13_2_of_transcendental`, `exercise_13_2_adjoin`, `exercise_13_2_ratFunc`) proves `Infinite (IntermediateField K L)` via the injective family $m \mapsto K(t^{m+1})$.
39. **Primitive element theorem, Galois splitting-field corollary, and `Exercise 13.5` (§13.2, printed pp. 108–109):** `theorem_13_2` (and `theorem_13_2_separable`) formalizes Garling's p. 108 proof through the splitting field $M$ of $g = \prod_i m_{\alpha_i}$ (`isGarlingSeparablePoly_prod_minpoly`, `isGarlingSplittingField_tower_of_generators`, `isGarlingGaloisExtension_of_splittingField`, `finite_intermediateField_of_isGarlingGaloisExtension`, and `theorem_13_1`). `corollary_to_theorem_13_2` (and `corollary_to_theorem_13_2_galois`) exhibits $f = \operatorname{minpoly}_K(\alpha)$ for a primitive element $L = K(\alpha)$. `exercise_13_5` (and `exercise_13_5_separable`) proves that if $L:K$ is finite separable and $M:L$ is finite simple ($M = L(\gamma)$, where $\gamma$ need not be separable over $K$), then $M:K$ is simple.
40. **Inseparable counterexample and projective-plane intermediate-field classification (`Exercise 13.3`, §13.2, printed p. 109):** `exercise_13_3` (`exercise_13_3_i`–`exercise_13_3_iv`) is proved polymorphically for any prime $p$ and any tower $\mathbb{Z}_p \to J = \mathbb{Z}_p(\alpha) \to K = J(\beta) \to L$ with $\alpha$ transcendental over $\mathbb{Z}_p$, $\beta$ transcendental over $J$, and `IsGarlingSplittingField K L ((X^p - C α)(X^p - C β))`, while `exercise_13_3_tower_exists` constructs a concrete witness tower (`RatFunc (ZMod p)` iterated twice plus `Polynomial.SplittingField`) for every prime $p$ so the hypotheses are kernel-proved non-vacuous. For part `(iv)` ($p = 2$, *"find all the intermediate fields $L:M:K$"*), `intermediateFieldEquivProjQuotOfSqMem` and `exercise_13_3_iv` prove the complete classification: every intermediate field $M$ satisfies either $M = \bot$ ($[M:K] = 1$), $M = \top$ ($[M:K] = 4$), or $[M:K] = 2$, and the proper non-trivial intermediate fields stand in canonical explicit bijection `[γ] ↦ K(γ)` (`intermediateFieldEquivProjQuotOfSqMem_mk`) with the projective plane $\mathbb{P}(K; L/K) \cong \mathbb{P}^2(K)$ (`Projectivization K (L ⧸ LinearMap.range (algebraMap K L))`, parameterized in the $K$-basis $(1, u, v, uv)$ with $u^2 = \alpha, v^2 = \beta$ by homogeneous lines $[c_1 : c_2 : c_3] \in \mathbb{P}^2(K)$ via $K(c_1 u + c_2 v + c_3 uv)$), and in particular form an infinite set.
41. **`Exercise 13.4` (`QUEUE.md` fidelity hold) and `§13.3 An example` (printed p. 109):** The printed `only if` (`⇒`) direction of `Exercise 13.4` — claiming that for a Galois extension $L:K$ with Galois group $\{\sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_n\}$, $L = K(\alpha)$ implies $(\sigma_1(\alpha), \dots, \sigma_n(\alpha))$ is a $K$-basis for $L$ — is mathematically false (confusing the normal basis condition with the primitive element / power basis condition: whenever $\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(\alpha) = \sum_{i=1}^n \sigma_i(\alpha) = 0$, the Galois orbit sums to $0$ over $K$ and is linearly dependent, and at $L = K, \alpha = 0$ the single vector $(0)$ is also linearly dependent). `Garling/Chapter13.lean` holds `Exercise 13.4` in `QUEUE.md` and kernel-proves: (i) the unconditional `if` (`⇐`) direction for both indexed `Module.Basis` (`exercise_13_4_if_basis`) and Chapter 1 subset `IsBasis` (`exercise_13_4_if_isBasis_set`); (ii) three explicit counterexamples to the printed `only if` direction (`exercise_13_4_only_if_counterexample_qSqrtTwo` on $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2}):\mathbb{Q}$ at $\alpha = \sqrt{2}$, `exercise_13_4_only_if_degenerate_counterexample` on $\mathbb{Q}:\mathbb{Q}$ at $\alpha = 0$, and `exercise_13_4_only_if_counterexample_biquadratic` on §13.3's own example $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2}, \sqrt{3}):\mathbb{Q}$ at $\alpha = \sqrt{2} + \sqrt{3}$); and (iii) the true companion characterizations (`exercise_13_4_iff_injective`, `exercise_13_4_iff_ncard_eq`, and `exercise_13_4_power_basis`). All results of the unnumbered worked example in `§13.3` on $f = x^4 - 5x^2 + 6 = (x^2 - 2)(x^2 - 3)$ and $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2}, \sqrt{3}):\mathbb{Q}$ (`example_13_3_poly_eq_mul`, `example_13_3_isSplittingField_and_galois`, `example_13_3_finrank_and_degree`, `example_13_3_action_table`, `example_13_3_galoisGroup_univ`, `example_13_3_galoisGroupIso`, `example_13_3_nontrivial_subgroups`, `example_13_3_fixedFields`, `example_13_3_primitive_element_iff`) are also formalized and kernel-proved.
42. **Literal §8.1 *algebraically closed* and *algebraic closure* predicates, constant/zero synchronization with Chapter 7, and `Theorem 8.1` `L = L'` formulations (`§8.1`, printed pp. 71–72):** Printed p. 71 defines $L$ to be *algebraically closed* if *"every $f$ in $L[x]$ splits over $L$"* (`IsGarlingAlgebraicallyClosed L := ∀ f : L[X], GarlingSplitsOver L L f`) and defines $L:K$ to be an *algebraic closure* of $K$ if $L:K$ is algebraic and $L$ is algebraically closed (`IsGarlingAlgebraicClosure`, `IsGarlingAlgebraicClosureTriple`, `IsGarlingAlgebraicClosureHom`). Because `GarlingSplitsOver L L f` from `Garling/Chapter07.lean` already handles $f = 0$ ($\lambda = 0$) and constants ($n = 0$, empty product $1$) uniformly, `isGarlingAlgebraicallyClosed_iff` and `isGarlingAlgebraicClosure_iff` prove exact equivalence with Mathlib's `IsAlgClosed L` and `IsAlgClosure K L` across **all** $f \in L[X]$ with zero side conditions (`complex_isGarlingAlgebraicallyClosed` and `complex_not_isGarlingAlgebraicClosure_rat` via `Exercise 4.7`). In `Theorem 8.1 (iii)` (*"$L:K$ is algebraic, and if $L':L$ is algebraic then $L = L'$"*), when $L':L$ is typed as an abstract `[Field L'] [Algebra L L']`, $L = L'$ corresponds equivalently (`bot_eq_top_iff_surjective_algebraMap`) to `(⊥ : IntermediateField L L') = ⊤` (`theorem_8_1`) and `Function.Surjective (algebraMap L L')` (`theorem_8_1_garling`, with universe-polymorphic `(ii) ⇒ (iii)` in `theorem_8_1_ii_imp_iii_univ` and `isGarlingAlgebraicallyClosed_surjective_of_algebraic`), while for subfields $K \le L \le L'$ inside an algebraically closed ambient field $\Omega$, `theorem_8_1_subfields` proves literal subfield equality `L = L'`. `corollary_to_theorem_8_1` and `garlingAlgebraicNumbers_isGarlingAlgebraicClosure` prove p. 72's `Corollary` ($L_a : K$) and its specialization $A : \mathbb{Q}$ (`GarlingAlgebraicNumbers`).
43. **Artin's polynomial-ring maximal-ideal construction in `Theorem 8.2` (`§8.2`, printed pp. 72–74):** Rather than wrapping Mathlib's `AlgebraicClosure K`, `Garling/Chapter08.lean` formalizes Garling's entire main-text proof of `Theorem 8.2`: indexing $U = \{(f, j) : f \in K[x] \text{ monic}, \deg f > 0, 1 \le j \le \deg f\}$ (`ArtinIndex K`), forming $K[X_U]$ (`ArtinPolyRing K = MvPolynomial (ArtinIndex K) K`), proving the elementary-symmetric Vieta expansion $g(f) = \prod_{j=1}^n (x - x_j(f)) = x^n - s_1(f)x^{n-1} + \dots + (-1)^n s_n(f)$ dropped by OCR on p. 73 (`artinG_coeff_and_artinT`), proving $1 \notin I = (t_i(f))$ (`artinIdeal_proper`) by constructing a finite splitting field for $h = f_1 \cdots f_N$ via Chapter 7's `theorem_7_3_natDegree` and evaluating $E : K[X_U] \to L$, enlarging $I$ to a maximal proper ideal $J$ via Chapter 3's `theorem_3_14` (`artinMaxIdeal`), forming the quotient field $M = K[X_U]/J$ in the same universe `u` as $K$ via Chapter 3's `theorem_3_16` (`ArtinAlgClosure K`), and proving $M = K(\{\beta_u : u \in U\})$ is algebraic over $K$ via Chapter 4's `corollary_2_to_theorem_4_6` and algebraically closed via `theorem_8_1` (`artinAlgClosure_isGarlingAlgebraicClosure`, `theorem_8_2`, `theorem_8_2_triple`).
44. **Bounded-carrier Zorn proof of algebraic closure and cross-part field structure on `j(K)` (`Exercises 8.1–8.3`, `§8.2`, printed pp. 74–75):** Every Zorn poset in Chapter 8 is provably non-empty, so Chapter 2's `[Nonempty S]`-guarded `zorn_lemma_set` applies directly without extra hypotheses (`(V, f)` in `exercise_8_1_ii` / `exercise_8_1_ii_subset`; empty partial injection in `exercise_8_2_i` across arbitrary universes `{K : Type u} {L : Type v}` into $U = K[x] \times \mathbb{Z}^+ = K[X] \times \mathbb{N}_+$; `ex83BaseTriple K` in `exercise_8_3_ii`; and `(⊥, i)` in `theorem_8_3`). In `Exercise 8.3`, part `(i)` constructs the injection $j : K \to P(U)$, $\alpha \mapsto \{(x - \alpha, 1)\}$ (`ex83J`, `ex83J_injective`), equips $j(K)$ (`Ex83JK K`) with the transported field structure `ex83JKField K` (`IsGarlingField` + `Field`), and proves `ex83JRingEquiv K : K ≃+* Ex83JK K` is a field isomorphism (`exercise_8_3_i`). Reading `Exercise 8.3` sequentially (cf. Note 22 on `Exercise 5.5`), condition `(ii)(c)` — *"$(i, j(K), F(S))$ is an algebraic extension (here $i$ is the inclusion mapping)"* — equips $j(K)$ with this exact field structure from `(i)` (`Ex83F`, using `garlingFieldToField` to convert `IsGarlingField t.S t.add t.mul` to `Field t.S`), `exercise_8_3_ii` proves `ex83Le` is a `IsGarlingPartialOrder` on $\mathscr{F}$ with a maximal element via `zorn_lemma_set`, and `exercise_8_3_iii` proves via `Theorem 7.3`, `Exercise 8.2 (ii)`, and `Theorem 8.1 (iii)` that every maximal element $(S, +, \cdot)$ of $\mathscr{F}$ yields an algebraic closure $(j, K, F(S))$ for $K$.
45. **Monomorphism extension, uniqueness, `Exercise 8.4` unique subfield identification, and characteristic-free `Exercises 8.5–8.6` (`§8.3`, printed pp. 75–76):** `theorem_8_3` and `theorem_8_3_triple` formalize Garling's pp. 75–76 Zorn + `Theorem 7.4` extension proof across arbitrary universes `{K₁ : Type u} {K₂ : Type v} {L : Type w}` without invoking Mathlib's `IsAlgClosed.lift`; `theorem_8_4`, `theorem_8_4_ringEquiv`, and `theorem_8_4_algEquiv` deduce uniqueness of algebraic closures from `Theorem 8.3` + `Theorem 8.1 (iii)`, followed by `GarlingAlgClosure K` ($\bar{K}:K$, p. 76). Following the canonical-identification convention of `Exercises 3.27, 4.1, 5.12, 13.3(iv)` (Notes 14, 19, 25, 40), `Exercise 8.4` (*"What is the algebraic closure of $\mathbb{Q}$ (as a subfield of $\mathbb{C}$)?"*) is proved both as `∀ F : Subfield ℂ, IsGarlingAlgebraicClosure ℚ F ↔ F = GarlingAlgebraicNumbers` and `∃! F : Subfield ℂ, IsGarlingAlgebraicClosure ℚ F` (`exercise_8_4_iff`, `exercise_8_4`), plus the `IntermediateField ℚ ℂ` counterpart (`exercise_8_4_intermediateField`). `exercise_8_5` proves `Infinite L ∧ ¬ Finite L ∧ setOrder L = ⊤` via Euclid's polynomial $1 + \prod_{a \in L} (x - a)$. `exercise_8_6` (`exercise_8_6_adjoin`, `exercise_8_6_ratFunc`) proves `¬ IsGarlingAlgebraicallyClosed L` for any simple transcendental extension $L = K(\alpha)$ in **every** characteristic (including $\operatorname{char} K = 2$) by invoking Chapter 7's `exercise_7_3_iff` at $n = 2$ ($x^2 - \alpha \in K(\alpha)[x]$ is irreducible of degree $2$, so $\alpha$ has no square root in $K(\alpha)$).
46. **Countable-field construction & methodological comparison (`Exercise 8.7`) and senses of `L̄ = K̄` (`Exercise 8.8`, `§8.3`, printed p. 76 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity holds), plus `§8.4` remark (printed p. 77):**
   - `Exercise 8.7`: Held in `QUEUE.md` for its constructive + methodological prompt (*"Show how to construct ... Is your construction less fallacious than the 'fallacious proof' of Theorem 8.2?"*). `Garling/Chapter08.lean` formalizes both rigorous resolutions of the p. 72 triple-vs-subset sleight-of-hand for any countable (finite or countably infinite) field $K$ **without** invoking Zorn's lemma: (a) iterating `theorem_7_3_natDegree` along any surjection `enumPoly : ℕ → K[X]` (`Ex87Tower`, `ex87Step`, `ex87Trans`) and forming the explicit directed-colimit quotient field `Ex87Colimit enumPoly` (`ex87Setoid`, `ex87ColimitField`, `exercise_8_7_of_surjective`), and (b) embedding the countable tower as an honest subset chain $j(K) = S_0 \subseteq S_1 \subseteq \dots \subseteq P(K[x] \times \mathbb{Z}^+)$ whose set-theoretic union $\bigcup_{n \in \mathbb{N}} S_n$ is an algebraic closure of $K$ (`exercise_8_7`).
   - `Exercise 8.8`: Held in `QUEUE.md` for its open-ended *"In what sense is it true that $\bar{L} = \bar{K}$?"* phrasing (since p. 76 defines $\bar{K}:K$ to denote *any* algebraic closure of $K$, so independent choices need not share an ambient carrier). `Garling/Chapter08.lean` kernel-proves all three exact senses (`exercise_8_8`): (1) **tower sense** (`exercise_8_8_tower_iff`): for $K \to L \to M$ with $L:K$ algebraic, `IsGarlingAlgebraicClosure L M ↔ IsGarlingAlgebraicClosure K M` (via `Theorem 4.7`); (2) **ambient-subfield sense** (`exercise_8_8_ambient_subfield_eq`): inside an algebraically closed $\Omega$, `algebraicSubfield L Ω = algebraicSubfield K Ω ↔ IsAlgebraicExtension K L`; and (3) **$K$-isomorphism sense** (`exercise_8_8_isomorphism`): for any algebraic $(i, K, L)$ and arbitrary algebraic closures $\bar{K}:K$, $\bar{L}:L$, there exist a monomorphism $\sigma : L \to \bar{K}$ fixing $K$ (`Theorem 8.3`) and an isomorphism $\tilde{\sigma} : \bar{L} \simeq \bar{K}$ extending $\sigma$ and fixing $K$ (`Theorem 8.4`); together with `exercise_8_8_non_algebraic_counterexample` (`IsEmpty (GarlingAlgebraicNumbers ≃+* ℂ)`, showing `IsAlgebraicExtension K L` cannot be dropped). Finally, `conclusions_8_4_complex_over_real` formalizes §8.4's p. 77 observation that $\mathbb{C}$ is both a splitting field of the single polynomial $x^2 + 1 \in \mathbb{R}[x]$ and an algebraic closure of $\mathbb{R}$.
47. **Literal §9.1 *normal* extension, *splitting field extension for `S ⊆ K[x]`*, synchronization with Chapter 7 at `f = 0` and `S = ∅`, and `Theorem 9.1` & `Corollary 1` (`§9.1`, printed pp. 78–80):** Printed p. 78 defines $L:K$ to be *normal* if $L:K$ is algebraic and every irreducible $f \in K[x]$ either splits over $L$ (`GarlingSplitsOver K L f`) or has no roots in $L$ (`∀ α : L, evalRingHom K L α f ≠ 0`), formalized as `IsGarlingNormal K L` (plus `IsGarlingNormalTriple`) and proved equivalent to Chapter 3's `IsGarlingIrreducible` + exclusive-`or` phrasing (`isGarlingNormal_iff_garlingIrreducible_xor`), to p. 78's minimal-polynomial characterization (`isGarlingNormal_iff_minpoly_splits`), and to Mathlib's `Normal K L` (`isGarlingNormal_iff`, invariant under $K$-isomorphism via `isGarlingNormal_congr`). Printed p. 78 defines $L:K$ to be a *splitting field extension for $S \subseteq K[x]$* if every $f \in S$ splits over $L$ and there is no proper intermediate subfield $L \supseteq L' \supseteq K$ over which every $f \in S$ splits (`IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet`, bridged to `Subfield L` via `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet_iff_subfield` and to Chapter 7's `IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtension` on singletons via `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet_singleton_iff`). Because $0 \in K[x]$ already splits over every extension under §7.1's `GarlingSplitsOver` ($\lambda = 0$), `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet_diff_zero_iff` proves `IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet K L S ↔ IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet K L (S \ {0})` and `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet_empty_iff` proves `IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet K L ∅ ↔ (⊥ : IntermediateField K L) = ⊤` ($L = K$). Consequently, `Theorem 9.1` (`theorem_9_1`, proved step-by-step via Garling's 6-arrow subfield diamond on pp. 78–79 by passing to $S \setminus \{0\}$ before forming the root set $A$ and finite non-zero products $g = f_1 \cdots f_n$ and applying `Theorem 7.5` / `Corollary 1` [to `Theorem 7.5`]) and `Corollary 1` [to `Theorem 9.1`] (both under `IsFiniteExtension K L` in `corollary_1_to_theorem_9_1` and as the unconditional equivalence `IsFiniteExtension K L ∧ IsGarlingNormal K L ↔ ∃ g : K[X], IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtension K L g` in `corollary_1_to_theorem_9_1_iff`, including $g = 0$) hold **literally as printed** with zero side conditions. By contrast, p. 78's unnumbered prose remark (*"If $S$ is a finite set $\{f_1, \dots, f_n\}$ then $L:K$ is a splitting field extension for $S$ iff it is a splitting field extension for $g = f_1 \cdots f_n$"*) requires $0 \notin S$ (`isGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet_range_iff_prod`, `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtensionSet_finset_iff_prod`), witnessed by `finite_set_prod_zero_mem_counterexample` ($S = \{x^2 + 1, 0\} \subseteq \mathbb{R}[x]$ has splitting field $\mathbb{C}$ whereas $(x^2 + 1) \cdot 0 = 0$ splits over $\mathbb{R}$), and p. 80's 3-line prose remark for $L = K(\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_n)$ (`normal_iff_splittingField_prod_minpoly_of_adjoin`) makes explicit that $\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_n$ are algebraic over $K$ (`∀ i, IsGarlingAlgebraic K (α i)`) so their minimal polynomials $m_i$ exist (§4.3, p. 44).
48. ***Normal closure* `F:L` vs. extension-triple formulations, `Corollaries 2–3`, and `Exercises 9.1–9.4` (`§§9.1–9.2`, printed pp. 80–81):** Printed p. 80 defines $F:L$ to be a *normal closure* for algebraic $L:K$ if $F:K$ is normal and every intermediate field $F : M : L$ (`M : IntermediateField L F`) with $M:K$ normal satisfies $M = F$ (`IsGarlingNormalClosure K L F` and `IsGarlingNormalClosureTriple`; `isGarlingNormalClosure_iff_of_tower` shows `IsAlgebraicExtension K L` is automatically entailed by `IsGarlingNormal K F` in any tower $K \to L \to F$, covering `Exercise 9.4` where algebraicity of $L:K$ is not restated; `isGarlingNormalClosure_surjective_of_tower` proves universe-polymorphic surjectivity `Function.Surjective (algebraMap M F)` for any abstract tower $K \to L \to M \to F$ with $M:K$ normal; and `isGarlingNormalClosure_iff_isNormalClosure` proves exact equivalence with Mathlib's `IsNormalClosure K L F`). Both `[Algebra K L]` and explicit §4.2 `FieldExtensionTriple` forms are proved for normal-closure existence and uniqueness: `corollary_2_to_theorem_9_1` and `corollary_2_to_theorem_9_1_triple` (via p. 80's basis-product construction and `Theorem 7.3`, proving both `IsFiniteExtension L F` and `IsFiniteExtension K F`), `exercise_9_1` and `exercise_9_1_triple` (via Chapter 8's `theorem_8_2` algebraic closure $\bar{L}$ and intersection of normal subfields containing $L$), and `exercise_9_4`, `exercise_9_4_ringEquiv`, `exercise_9_4_algEquiv`, and `exercise_9_4_triple` (via `Theorem 8.3` + `Exercise 9.3` across arbitrary universes). `Corollary 3` [to `Theorem 9.1`] (p. 80) is proved for arbitrary normal extensions (no finiteness assumption) both for `M : IntermediateField K L` (`corollary_3_to_theorem_9_1`) and for any scalar tower $K \to M \to L$ across arbitrary universes (`corollary_3_to_theorem_9_1_tower`). `Exercise 9.2` (p. 80) is proved via closure of normal intermediate fields under arbitrary suprema (`isGarlingNormal_sSup`), holding both under `IsAlgebraicExtension K L` (`exercise_9_2`) and unconditionally (`exercise_9_2_unconditional`). `Exercise 9.3` (p. 80, where l. 1 omits *"is an extension"* after $L:K$ and $K(M_1, M_2)$ denotes `M₁ ⊔ M₂ = garlingFieldGenSubfield K (M₁ ∪ M₂)`) is proved unconditionally in `exercise_9_3` without requiring $L:K$ itself to be algebraic.
49. **Characteristic-free `Theorem 9.2` and `Exercise 9.5`, plus §9.2's `ℚ(2^{1/3}, ω)` example (`§9.2`, printed pp. 80–81):** The opening paragraph of §9.2 (p. 80) illustrates failure of lower-step normality on $\mathbb{Q}(2^{1/3}):\mathbb{Q}$ inside $\mathbb{Q}(2^{1/3}, \omega):\mathbb{Q}$ ($x^3 = 2, \omega^3 = 1$, implicitly taking a non-trivial cube root $\omega \ne 1$): `section_9_2_cube_root_example` proves both that $\mathbb{Q}(2^{1/3}, \omega):\mathbb{Q}$ (`omegaCubeRoot` from Chapter 7) is a splitting field of $x^3 - 2$ and normal while $\mathbb{Q}(2^{1/3}):\mathbb{Q}$ is not normal, and that at $\omega = 1$ the extension $\mathbb{Q}(2^{1/3}, 1) = \mathbb{Q}(2^{1/3}):\mathbb{Q}$ is not normal. `Theorem 9.2` (p. 81; `(iii)` prints `automorphsim`) is proved in full characteristic-free generality (no separability needed) both for `AlgEquiv` automorphisms `σ : L ≃ₐ[K] L` (`theorem_9_2`) and for Chapter 4's `IsFieldAutomorphism τ ∧ FixesBaseField K L τ` (`theorem_9_2_garling`, bridged via `forall_algEquiv_iff_isFieldAutomorphism`), while `normal_intermediateField_imp_aut_subset_and_eq` proves `(i) ⇒ (ii) ∧ (iii)` without requiring $L:K$ to be finite or normal. `Exercise 9.5` (p. 81, where $\sigma(g) = h$ uses §7.2's `garlingMapPoly σ = Polynomial.mapRingHom σ`) is proved in full characteristic-free generality (whether or not $f \in K[x]$ is monic or has roots in $L$) in both `AlgEquiv` (`exercise_9_5`) and Chapter 4 `IsFieldAutomorphism` (`exercise_9_5_garling`) form by adjoining a splitting field $E:L$ of $f \cdot p \in K[x]$ via `Theorem 7.3`, mapping a root of $g$ to a root of $h$ via `Corollary 1` [to `Theorem 7.4`] and `Corollary 1` [to `Theorem 7.5`], and restricting from $E$ to $L$ via `Theorem 9.2`.
50. **Algebraic-closure monomorphisms `j : L → L̄` in `Exercise 9.6` (OCR overbar warning) and infinite-degree `Exercise 9.7` (`§9.2`, printed p. 81):** Visually verified on the 200 DPI render of printed p. 81 (PDF p. 89), `Exercise 9.6 (ii)` and `(iii)` quantify over monomorphisms $j$ from $L$ to **$\bar{L}$** fixing $K$ (whereas `garling1986_pages.txt` strips the overbar to $L$, which by `Theorem 4.8` would make `(iii)` hold for *every* algebraic extension $L:K$). Identifying $L \subseteq \bar{L}$ with `(algebraMap L LBar).range` per §4.2 (p. 40) while $j$ ranges over all $K$-fixing ring homomorphisms `L →+* LBar` (`j.comp (algebraMap K L) = algebraMap K LBar`), `exercise_9_6` proves `(i) ↔ (ii) ↔ (iii)` for **any** algebraic closure `IsGarlingAlgebraicClosure L LBar` across arbitrary universes `{K : Type u} {L : Type v} {LBar : Type w}` (using Chapter 4's `theorem_4_8` for `(ii) ⇒ (iii)` on infinite algebraic extensions), `exercise_9_6_garling` proves the specialized statement on Chapter 8's canonical `GarlingAlgClosure L` (via `theorem_8_4_algEquiv`), and `exercise_9_6_subfield` proves the ambient-subfield form `L : IntermediateField K LBar`. Following Garling's hint *(Use the previous exercise and Theorem 8.3.)*, `exercise_9_7` (`AlgEquiv`) and `exercise_9_7_garling` (`IsFieldAutomorphism`) drop `IsFiniteExtension K L` from `Theorem 9.2` by extending any $K$-fixing monomorphism $j : M \to \bar{L}$ along $M \hookrightarrow L$ via Chapter 8's `theorem_8_3` and restricting the resulting $K$-automorphism of $L$ via `exercise_9_6`.
51. **Literal §10.1 *separable* polynomial/element/extension predicates vs. Mathlib `Polynomial.Separable`, zero/unit edge behavior, and zero-algebraic-closure proofs (`§10.1`, printed p. 82):** Printed p. 82 defines an irreducible polynomial $f \in K[x]$ of degree $n$ to be *separable (over $K$)* if it has $n$ distinct roots in a splitting field extension $L:K$ (`IsGarlingSeparableIrreducible f`, independent of the choice of splitting field by `Theorem 7.5` in `isGarlingSeparableIrreducible_iff_of_splittingField` and proved equivalent to `f.Separable` for irreducible $f$ in `isGarlingSeparableIrreducible_iff_separable`), and defines an *arbitrary* polynomial $f \in K[x]$ to be *separable (over $K$)* *"if each of its irreducible factors is separable"* (`IsGarlingSeparablePoly f`, bridged to Chapter 3's `IsGarlingIrreducible` in `isGarlingSeparablePoly_iff_garlingIrreducible`). Unlike Mathlib's squarefree-requiring `Polynomial.Separable` (`mathlib_separable_iff_squarefree_and_isGarlingSeparablePoly`: `f.Separable ↔ f ≠ 0 ∧ Squarefree f ∧ IsGarlingSeparablePoly f`), Garling's `IsGarlingSeparablePoly` admits non-zero units (`isGarlingSeparablePoly_of_isUnit`) and repeated separable irreducible factors (`repeated_linear_factor_garlingSeparable_not_mathlibSeparable` on $(x - 1)^2 \in \mathbb{Q}[x]$), and at $f = 0$ satisfies `IsGarlingSeparablePoly (0 : K[X]) ↔ ∀ f : K[X], IsGarlingSeparablePoly f` (`isGarlingSeparablePoly_zero_iff`: every irreducible $g \in K[x]$ divides $0$). Synchronized with Chapter 7's `isGarlingSplittingFieldExtension_zero_iff` ($L = K$ at $f = 0$), `Corollary 3` [to `Theorem 10.3`], `Corollary` [to `Theorem 10.6`], `Corollary` [to `Theorem 10.8`], and `Exercise 10.7` all hold **literally for every `f : K[X]` (including `f = 0`, units, and repeated separable factors)**. `Garling/Chapter10.lean` never imports `Garling.Chapter08` and uses **zero** algebraic closures anywhere (`isGarlingSeparableElement_tower_top`, `theorem_10_1`, and `theorem_10_1_tower` prove `Theorem 10.1` directly via Chapter 7's `theorem_7_3_natDegree`).
52. **Monomorphism counting, prefix fields `K(α₁, ..., α_{i-1})`, `Exercise 10.1` normal-closure tower (OCR `L'` → `E` warning), and finiteness in *Galois extension* (`§§10.2–10.3`, printed pp. 83–85):** Following §7.2 (p. 63), `j(m_α)` denotes `garlingMapPoly j (minpoly K α) = (minpoly K α).map j`. `theorem_10_2` (`IsSimpleExtension K Kα`) and `theorem_10_2_adjoin` ($K(\alpha) \subseteq M$), `theorem_10_3` (inducting on `[K' : K]` via `theorem_10_2` across arbitrary universes `{K : Type u} {K' : Type v} {L : Type w}`), `corollary_1_to_theorem_10_3` (formalizing $K(\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_{i-1})$ at $i = 1$ as `garlingPrefixField K α 0 = ⊥`, i.e., $K_0 = K$), `corollary_2_to_theorem_10_3` (via `Theorem 4.6`), `corollary_3_to_theorem_10_3` (including $f = 0$), and `corollary_4_to_theorem_10_3` / `corollary_4_to_theorem_10_3_tower` (building the normal closure via Chapter 9's `corollary_2_to_theorem_9_1` with zero algebraic closures) are all proved literally. Visually verified on the 160 DPI scan of p. 84 (PDF p. 92; `garling1986_pages.txt` corrupts both occurrences of $L'$ to $E$), `Exercise 10.1` quantifies over $K$-fixing monomorphisms $L \to L'$ into a normal closure $L':L$ of finite $L:K$, proved across arbitrary universes in `exercise_10_1` (`RingHom`), `exercise_10_1_algHom` (`L →ₐ[K] L'`), and `exercise_10_1_triple` (`FieldExtensionTriple`). In §10.3 (p. 84), Garling defines $L:K$ to be a *Galois extension* if it is **finite**, normal, and separable (`IsGarlingGaloisExtension K L`, `IsGarlingGaloisExtensionTriple`, bridged via `isGarlingGaloisExtension_iff : IsGarlingGaloisExtension K L ↔ IsFiniteExtension K L ∧ IsGalois K L`), and `theorem_10_4` / `theorem_10_4_algEquiv` prove the exact characterization via $|\operatorname{Aut}_K(L)| = [L:K]$ (`Theorem 4.8` + `Theorem 10.3` + `Exercise 10.1` + `Corollary 2` [to `Theorem 9.1`]).
53. **Formal differentiation `D`, `Theorem 10.5` (`QUEUE.md` fidelity hold on p. 85's `in K[x]` slip), `Theorem 10.6` + `Corollary`, Taylor's formula (`Exercise 10.2`), and `Exercise 10.3` (`QUEUE.md` fidelity hold on omitted `= 0`) (`§10.4`, printed pp. 85–87):** `garlingDeriv : K[X] → K[X]` ($\sum n a_n x^{n-1}$) is bridged to `Polynomial.derivative` (`garlingDeriv_eq_derivative`) alongside its $K$-linear map `garlingDerivLinearMap`, Leibniz rule `garlingDeriv_mul`, power rule `garlingDeriv_pow`, and characteristic-$p$ rule `garlingDeriv_X_pow_char` ($\mathrm{D} x^p = 0$). Two §10.4 items are held in `QUEUE.md`:
   - `Theorem 10.5` (pp. 85–86): part `(i)` (*"$f$ has a repeated root in $L$"*) invokes p. 85's prose definition, which prints `"$(x - \alpha)^k \mid f$ in $K[x]$"` instead of `"in $L[x]$"` (contradicted on p. 86 l. 1 by `"$f = (x - \alpha)^k g$, where $k > 1$ and $g \in L[x]$"`). `theorem_10_5_literal_p85_counterexample` refutes `(ii) ⇒ (i)` and `(iii) ⇒ (i)` under the literal p. 85 `in K[x]` wording on $f = (x^2 - 2)^2 \in \mathbb{Q}[x]$ in $L = \mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2})$ (`qSqrtTwoSubfield`), while `theorem_10_5` proves the complete 3-way equivalence `(i) ↔ (ii) ↔ (iii)` under p. 86's `L[x]` divisibility `HasGarlingRepeatedRoot K L f`.
   - `Exercise 10.3` (p. 87): the displayed formula omits **`= 0`** at the end of $f(\alpha) = \mathrm{D}f(\alpha) = \dots = \mathrm{D}^{r-1}f(\alpha)$, and *exact multiplicity* is not defined earlier in Chapter 10's prose. `exercise_10_3_omitted_zero_counterexamples` refutes the literal `= 0`-omitted right-hand side both at $r = 1$ ($f = x, \alpha = 1 \in \mathbb{Q}$) and at $r = 2$ ($f = x^2 + x + 1, \alpha = 0 \in \mathbb{Q}$), while `exercise_10_3` ($1 \le r \le n$, `IsRootOfExactMultiplicity`), `exercise_10_3_all_r` ($0 \le r \le n$, `HasExactRootMultiplicity`, bridged to `Polynomial.rootMultiplicity` via `hasExactRootMultiplicity_iff_rootMultiplicity`), and `exercise_10_3_extension` ($\alpha \in L$) prove all repaired forms from Taylor's formula (`exercise_10_2`, `exercise_10_2_le`). `Theorem 10.6` (`theorem_10_6`, `theorem_10_6_expand`) and its `Corollary` (`corollary_to_theorem_10_6`, `corollary_to_theorem_10_6_charZero`) hold literally as printed.
54. **Frobenius monomorphism, Wilson's theorem (`Exercise 10.4`), and primes in `ℤ + iℤ` (`Exercises 10.5–10.6`) (`§10.5`, printed pp. 87–88):** `garlingFrobenius` constructs $\phi(\alpha) = \alpha^p$ directly from Garling's p. 87 binomial divisibility argument (`prime_dvd_choose_between` via $r!(p-r)!\binom{p}{r} = p!$), `theorem_10_7` (and `theorem_10_7_charP`) proves `IsGarlingRingHom`, `IsRingMonomorphism`, and `{α : K | φ α = α} = primeSubfield K` via `Theorem 3.17` and degree-$p$ root counting on $x^p - x$, and `corollary_to_theorem_10_7` deduces `IsFieldAutomorphism` in 3 lines from `Theorem 10.7` + `Theorem 4.8` (`theorem_4_8_subfield`). In `Exercise 10.4` (p. 87, printed `shows that`), `exercise_10_4_factorization` proves $x^{p-1} - 1 = \prod_{a \in \mathbb{Z}_p \setminus \{0\}} (x - a)$ from `Theorem 10.7` and `exercise_10_4` evaluates at $0$ to establish $(p - 1)! + 1 \equiv 0 \pmod p$ in `ZMod p`, `Int.ModEq`, and `Nat.ModEq`. `Exercises 10.5` and `10.6` prove both Chapter 3's `IsGarlingPrime` on `GaussianIntSubring` ($\mathbb{Z} + \mathrm{i}\mathbb{Z} \subseteq \mathbb{C}$) and `Prime` on `GaussianInt` (`gaussianIntToSubring`, `gaussianIntSubringEquiv`), constructing $k = (2n)!$ in `exercise_10_5` directly from `exercise_10_4` so $k^2 + 1 \equiv 0 \pmod p$ and deducing $u^2 + v^2 = p$.
55. **Monic `f(x) = g(x^p)` irreducibility (`Theorem 10.8`), *perfect* and *totally inseparable* extensions, and prime-power root multiplicity `p^n` (`§10.6`, printed pp. 88–90):** In `Theorem 10.8` (p. 88), Garling's displayed formula $f(x) = a_0 + a_1 x^p + \dots + a_{n-1} x^{(n-1)p} + x^{np} = g(x^p)$ explicitly specifies monicity ($a_n = 1 = 1^p$, and $g.\text{coeff } i = 0 = 0^p$ for $i > n$), making *"not all coefficients of $g$ are $p$th powers"* coincide identically across $i < n$, $i \le n$, and $i \in \mathbb{N}$ (even at $n = 0$, where $f = g = 1$ is reducible on both sides); both `theorem_10_8` (monic `f = Polynomial.expand K p g`) and `theorem_10_8_sum` (explicit `Fin n`-indexed sum) are proved directly via Garling's p. 88 Bézout/derivative case split. `corollary_to_theorem_10_8` proves separability of every $f \in K[x]$ over any positive-characteristic field algebraic over its prime subfield, and `section_10_6_inseparable_example` formalizes p. 89's prose example $x^p - \alpha$ over $K = \mathbb{Z}_p(\alpha) = \operatorname{FractionRing}(\mathbb{Z}_p[X])$ ($\alpha = \text{ratFuncVar}(\mathbb{Z}_p)$). `IsGarlingPerfectField K` (`∀ f : K[X], IsGarlingSeparablePoly f`, bridged to Mathlib's `PerfectField K` in `isGarlingPerfectField_iff_perfectField`), `exercise_10_7`, `exercise_10_8` (using `Theorem 4.6` and `Theorem 7.2`, plus universe-polymorphic `(⇒)` in `exercise_10_8_mp_univ`), `exercise_10_9` ($f(x) = g(x^{p^n})$), `IsGarlingTotallyInseparable` + `exercise_10_10` ($\operatorname{minpoly}_K(\beta) = x^{p^n} - \alpha$, covering both $\beta \notin K$ and $\beta \in K$ at $n = 0$), and `exercise_10_11` (exact root multiplicity $p^n$ in $L[x]$; visually verified on `ch10-098.png` against `garling1986_pages.txt`'s `p^n` → `pn` corruption, and proved via `Exercise 10.9` since the parenthetical hint *(Hint: use Exercise 10.3.)* does not apply directly when $n \ge 1$ and $\deg f \ge p = \operatorname{char} K$) are all kernel-proved literally as printed.

56. **Automorphism group `Aut L`, polarity `(φ, γ)`, trajectories `T(λ)`, Artin's theorem (`Theorem 11.3`), and the Galois-extension dichotomy (`Theorem 11.4`) (`§11.1`, printed pp. 91–94):** Following Chapter 11's unnumbered preamble (p. 91) and §4.2's subfield convention, `GarlingAut L` (`L ≃+* L`, bridged to Chapter 4's `IsFieldAutomorphism` in `garlingAutEquivIsFieldAutomorphism`), the fixed-field map `garlingPhi : Set (GarlingAut L) → Subfield L`, and the Galois-group map `garlingGamma : Subfield L → Subgroup (GarlingAut L)` are defined for **any** subset $A \subseteq \operatorname{Aut} L$ and **any** subfield $K \subseteq L$ (without restricting to Galois extensions), alongside general `[Algebra K L]` companions `garlingGaloisSubgroup K L` and `GarlingGaloisGroup K L := L ≃ₐ[K] L` (`garlingGaloisGroupMulEquiv`, `garlingGaloisGroupSubfieldMulEquiv`). `theorem_11_1` and `theorem_11_1_algebra` prove `(i)`–`(iv)` literally as printed (noting the minor p. 91 grammar slip `an subset`), with inclusion-reversal proved in `garlingPhi_anti` and `garlingGamma_anti`, and `corollary_to_theorem_11_1` proves `φ(A) = φ(⟨A⟩)`. For any subgroup $G \le \operatorname{Aut} L$ with fixed field $K = \phi(G)$, `garlingTrajectory G : L → (G → L)` (`T(λ)(σ) = σ(λ)`) is injective (`garlingTrajectory_injective`), so `(i) ↔ (ii) ↔ (iii)` of `Theorem 11.2` holds literally both at the §1.3 `Set`-level (`IsLinearlyIndependent K B ↔ IsLinearlyIndependent K (T '' B) ↔ IsLinearlyIndependent L (T '' B)`, `theorem_11_2`, valid for **arbitrary** possibly-infinite $B \subseteq L$) and in `LinearIndepOn` form (`theorem_11_2_linearIndepOn`). `theorem_11_3` and `theorem_11_3_set_finite` prove Artin's theorem (`IsFiniteExtension (garlingPhi G) L`, `|G| = [L:φ(G)]`, `G = γ(φ(G))`, and `IsGarlingGaloisExtension (garlingPhi G) L` — finite + normal + separable), and `theorem_11_4` / `theorem_11_4_algebra` prove the exact `=` / `<` and `=` / `⊃` dichotomy for any finite extension $L:K$.
57. **Orbit-size characterization of simple Galois extensions (`Exercise 11.1`), Dedekind–Artin endomorphism basis (`Exercise 11.2`), basis-determinant criterion (`Exercise 11.3`), and trace/coset basis (`Exercise 11.4`) (`§11.1`, printed p. 94):** In `Exercise 11.1` (p. 94), *"the images of $\alpha$ under $G$ are all distinct"* means injectivity of the evaluation map $\sigma \mapsto \sigma(\alpha)$ on $G = \Gamma(L:K)$ (equivalently $|\{\sigma(\alpha) : \sigma \in G\}| = |G|$); `exercise_11_1` (`IntermediateField.adjoin K {α} = ⊤ ↔ Function.Injective (fun σ : GarlingGaloisGroup K L => σ α)`), `exercise_11_1_ncard`, and `exercise_11_1_subfield` prove all forms literally. In `Exercise 11.2`, `GarlingEnd K L := L →ₗ[K] L` models $\operatorname{End}_K(L)$ with left scalar multiplication $(\lambda \cdot T)(x) = \lambda(T x)$; `linearIndependent_aut_over_top` proves Dedekind independence of $\Gamma(L:K)$ over $L$ for **any** extension $L:K$, and `exercise_11_2` deduces $K$-linear independence of $\Gamma(L:K)$ in `GarlingEnd K L` (`IsLinearlyIndependent K` and `LinearIndependent K`) for any extension $L:K$. In `Exercise 11.3` (visually verified on `ch11-102.png` against `garling1986_pages.txt`'s dropped-$j$ OCR corruption `det(σ_i(β_j)) ≠ 0`), `G = {σ_1, …, σ_n}` enumerates $G$ without repetition (`σ : Fin n ≃ GarlingGaloisGroup K L`, $n = |G| = [L:K]$); `exercise_11_3` proves both `(∃ B : Module.Basis (Fin n) K L, ∀ j, B j = β j) ↔ Matrix.det (fun i j => σ i (β j)) ≠ 0` and `IsBasis K L (Set.range β) ↔ Matrix.det (fun i j => σ i (β j)) ≠ 0`, and `exercise_11_3_surjective_enum` extends the equivalence to any surjective `σ : Fin n → G` (both sides being false when `σ` repeats an element). `exercise_11_4_subfield` and `exercise_11_4` prove over `char K = 0` that for any subgroup $H \le G$ with fixed field $M = \phi(H)$ and $K$-basis $(\beta_1, \dots, \beta_n)$ of $L$, the relative traces $\gamma_j = \sum_{\sigma \in H} \sigma(\beta_j)$ generate $M = K(\gamma_1, \dots, \gamma_n)$ over $K$.
58. **Polynomial Galois group `Γ_K(f)`, `Theorem 11.5` (`f = 0` hold), `Theorem 11.6`, transitive root action, and `Exercises 11.5–11.7` (`§11.2`, printed pp. 94–96):** `GarlingPolyGaloisGroup K L f` (`L ≃ₐ[K] L` for a splitting field extension $L:K$ of $f$) and `garlingPolyGaloisSubgroup K L f` formalize $\Gamma_K(f)$, and `garlingPolyGaloisGroup_independent_of_splittingField` proves independence of the splitting-field choice up to `≃*` via `Corollary 1` to `Theorem 7.5`. `Theorem 11.5` is held in `QUEUE.md` because at $f = 0$ over an imperfect field $K = \operatorname{FractionRing}(\mathbb{Z}_2[X])$, $L = K$ satisfies $|\Gamma(0)| = 1 = [K:K]$ and $\phi(\Gamma(0)) = K$ while $\neg\operatorname{IsGarlingSeparablePoly}\ 0$ (`theorem_11_5_zero_imperfect_counterexample`); `theorem_11_5`, `theorem_11_5_subfield`, `theorem_11_5_unconditional`, and `theorem_11_5_perfect` prove all `f ≠ 0`, unconditional (`f = 0 ∨ IsGarlingSeparablePoly f`), and perfect-field versions. `theorem_11_6` proves that the root-restriction map `garlingRootPermHom K L f : GarlingPolyGaloisGroup K L f →* Equiv.Perm (garlingRootSet K L f)` is an injective group homomorphism literally for **all** $f \in K[X]$ (including $f = 0$, where `garlingRootSet K L 0 = Set.univ`). For the unnumbered post-proof prose on p. 95, `irreducible_imp_galoisGroup_actsTransitively` proves transitivity (`GarlingActsTransitively`) for every irreducible $f$, while `irreducible_of_monic_distinct_roots_transitive` proves the monic degree-$n$ distinct-roots converse for $n \ge 1$ (paired with `monic_zero_deg_not_irreducible_counterexample` at $n = 0$, $f = 1$). `Exercises 11.5–11.7` are held in `QUEUE.md` as `"Describe..."` / `"Find..."` prompts, with complete conjugacy classifications of transitive subgroups of $\Sigma_3$ (`A_3, Σ_3`), $\Sigma_4$ (`V_4, C_4, D_4, A_4, Σ_4`), and $\Sigma_5$ (`C_5, D_5, F_{20}, A_5, Σ_5`) proved in `exercise_11_5` and exact Galois-group isomorphisms proved universally across all splitting fields in `exercise_11_6` ($x^4 - 2$: $\mathbb{Q} \mapsto D_4$, $\mathbb{Z}_3 \mapsto C_2$, $\mathbb{Z}_7 \mapsto C_2$) and `exercise_11_7` ($x^4 + 2$: $\mathbb{Q} \mapsto D_4$, $\mathbb{Z}_3 \mapsto C_2$, $\mathbb{Z}_5 \mapsto C_4$).
59. **Prime-degree transposition generation, `"2 complex roots in ℂ"` in `Theorem 11.7`, `$x^5 - 4x + 2$` worked example, and `Exercise 11.8` (`§11.3`, printed pp. 96–97):** `transitive_subgroup_eq_top_of_prime_of_exists_swap` and `transitive_subgroup_eq_top_of_prime_of_exists_isSwap` formalize p. 96's unnumbered prose lemma that any transitive subgroup of $\Sigma_X$ of prime cardinality $|X| = p$ containing a transposition equals $\Sigma_X$ (explicitly handling the $x = z$ reflexivity case omitted in p. 96's `(x, y)(y, z)(x, y) = (x, z)` step). In `Theorem 11.7` (p. 96), *"2 complex roots in $\mathbb{C}$"* means **2 non-real complex roots** (`{z ∈ garlingRootSet ℚ ℂ f | z.im ≠ 0}.ncard = 2`, alongside $p - 2$ real roots, `separable_rat_roots_partition`), and *"is $\Sigma_p$"* means `Function.Bijective (garlingRootPermHom ℚ L f)` and `Nonempty (GarlingPolyGaloisGroup ℚ L f ≃* Equiv.Perm (Fin p))`; `theorem_11_7` and `theorem_11_7_of_real_roots` prove this across **all** primes $p$ (including $p = 2$, which has $0$ real roots and $2$ non-real roots) for **any** splitting field $L:\mathbb{Q}$ of $f$ (transporting complex conjugation on $\mathbb{Q}(\text{roots}) \subseteq \mathbb{C}$ via `Corollary 1` to `Theorem 7.5`). `section_11_3_quintic_example` proves irreducibility, exact $3$-real + $2$-non-real root counts (via IVT `exists_Ioo_root_of_mul_neg` and Rolle `ncard_real_roots_le_succ_of_deriv_bound`), and $\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(x^5 - 4x + 2) \cong \Sigma_5$. `Exercise 11.8` is held in `QUEUE.md` as a `"Sketch ... determine..."` prompt, with `exercise_11_8` proving for every $r \in \mathbb{N}$ (including $r = 0$) the zeros and alternating-sign profile of $f_r$, $|f_r(k)| \ge 5$ for all odd $k \in \mathbb{Z}$, Eisenstein irreducibility of $f_r - 2$, exact $(2r + 1)$-real / $2$-non-real root counts (bounding real roots of $f_r'$ via its two purely imaginary roots in $i(-2, 0)$ and $i(0, 2)$), and $\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(f_r - 2) \cong \Sigma_{2r+3}$ whenever $2r + 3$ is prime.
60. **Fundamental Theorem of Galois Theory for arbitrary finite extensions (`Theorem 11.8`), inverse Galois for finite groups (`Exercise 11.9`), and intersection of Galois subfields (`Exercise 11.10`) (`§11.4`, printed pp. 97–99):** Remarkably, Garling states and proves `Theorem 11.8` (p. 97) for **any finite extension** $L:K$ (without assuming $L:K$ is normal or separable) by setting $G = \Gamma(L:K)$ and $K_0 = \phi(G)$, so $L:K_0$ is automatically Galois with $\Gamma(L:K_0) = G$ by `Theorem 11.3`. On printed p. 97, `Γ(L, M)` and `Γ(φ(H), K_0))` (with an extra `)`) are typographical slips for `Γ(L:M)` and `Γ(φ(H):K_0)` (and p. 98 writes `Γ(φ(H):K)` / `Γ(M:K)` where `K_0` is required). Promoting `K_0 ≤ M` via `subfieldToIntermediateField` avoids Subfield-vs-Algebra typeclass diamonds while keeping exact subfield lattice identity; `theorem_11_8` and `theorem_11_8_algebra` prove all parts `(i)`–`(iii)`, including the order-reversing bijection `garlingFTGPhi K` / `garlingFTGGamma K`, the normality criterion $H \trianglelefteq G \iff \phi(H):K_0 \text{ normal}$, surjectivity and kernel `H` of the restriction map `garlingFTGRestrictionHom`, and `GarlingGaloisGroup K_0 φ(H) ≃* (G ⧸ H.subgroupOf G)`. `exercise_11_9_subfield` and `exercise_11_9` realize every finite group `G : Type u` as a Galois group inside the **same** universe `Type u` via $L = \operatorname{FractionRing}(\operatorname{MvPolynomial} G \mathbb{Q})$ and $K = \phi(G)$. `exercise_11_10` proves $L : K_1 \cap K_2$ is Galois iff $G = \langle G_1 \cup G_2 \rangle = G_1 \sqcup G_2$ is finite (relying essentially on finiteness in §10.3's `IsGarlingGaloisExtension`), with $G = \Gamma(L : K_1 \cap K_2)$.
61. **Unbound exponent `n` and `IsAlgClosed`-free Fundamental Theorem of Algebra (`Exercise 11.11`) and characteristic-`2` odd-irreducible counterexample (`Exercise 11.12`) (`§11.4`, printed p. 99):** `Exercise 11.11` is held in `QUEUE.md` because $n$ is unbound in `(i)`–`(ii)` (`exercise_11_11_unbound_n_counterexamples` refutes both universal $n$ and $n = \deg f$ on $K = \mathbb{R}, L = M = \mathbb{C}, f = x^2 + 1$). `exercise_11_11` proves `(i)`–`(iii)` across arbitrary universes `u, v, w` without any extra characteristic-$0$ assumption (proving odd-characteristic $K$ perfect via `Exercise 10.7`), and `exercise_11_11_iv_complex_algebraically_closed` proves `(iv)` `IsGarlingAlgebraicallyClosed ℂ` strictly by instantiating `(iii)` at $K = \mathbb{R}, L = \mathbb{C}$ via `complex_exists_sq_eq` and `real_odd_natDegree_exists_root`, verified at the kernel constant level to have **zero** transitive reference to `Complex.isAlgClosed`, `IsAlgClosed.splits`, or `complex_isGarlingAlgebraicallyClosed`. `Exercise 11.12` is held in `QUEUE.md` because the literal wording *"splitting field of all polynomials of odd degree over $\mathbb{Z}_2$"* includes $x \cdot f$ for every positive even-degree $f \in \mathbb{Z}_2[x]$, forcing $K$ to be already algebraically closed with **no** degree-$2$ extension $L:K$ (`exercise_11_12_literal_all_odd_is_algClosed`); `exercise_11_12` constructs inside Chapter 8's algebraic closure $\Omega$ of $\mathbb{Z}_2$ the splitting field $K = \{x \in \Omega : \exists d \text{ odd},\, x^{2^d} = x\}$ of all **irreducible** odd-degree polynomials over $\mathbb{Z}_2$ and $L = K(\omega)$ ($\omega^2 + \omega + 1 = 0$), proving $[L:K] = 2$, square roots in $L$, odd-degree roots in $K$, and $\neg\operatorname{IsGarlingAlgebraicallyClosed} L$ ($x^4 + x + 1$ has no root in $L$).
62. **Theorem on natural irrationalities (`Theorem 11.9`) and separable closing remark (`§11.5`, printed pp. 99–100):** `section_11_5_intermediate_example` formalizes p. 99's motivating example ($F:L:K \implies \Gamma_L(f) = \Gamma(F:L) \subseteq \Gamma(F:K) = \Gamma_K(f)$). `Theorem 11.9` is held in `QUEUE.md` because at $f = 0$ over an infinite extension $N = L = \operatorname{FractionRing}(\mathbb{Q}[X])$ of $K = \mathbb{Q}$, the root set `garlingRootSet ℚ N 0 = Set.univ` is infinite (admitting no finite tuple $\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_n$) and generates $M = N \ne \mathbb{Q}$, which is not a splitting field for $0$ over $\mathbb{Q}$ (`theorem_11_9_zero_counterexample`). For all $f \ne 0$ (including non-zero constants $n = 0$), `theorem_11_9` proves across arbitrary universes `u, v, w` the existence of the finite root tuple generating $M = K(\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_n)$, `IsGarlingSplittingFieldExtension K M f`, `Γ(M : L_0 ∩ M) ≤ Γ_K(f)`, injectivity and range `Γ(M : L_0 ∩ M)` of `θ : σ ↦ σ|_M`, and `Γ_L(f) ≃* Γ(M : L_0 ∩ M)` (following p. 100's proof via `Theorem 11.3`), and `theorem_11_9_separable` formalizes p. 100's closing remark (`f` separable $\implies L = L_0$, `Γ_L(f) ≃* Γ(M : L ∩ M)`, and `[N:L] = [M : L ∩ M]`).
63. **Finite fields, prime-power cardinality deduction, and `Theorem 12.1` + `Corollaries 1–2` (`§12.1`, printed pp. 101–102; zero algebraic closures):** `Garling/Chapter12.lean` imports only `Garling.Chapter01`, `Chapter03`, `Chapter04`, `Chapter07`, `Chapter09`, `Chapter10`, and `Chapter11` (zero Chapter 8 algebraic-closure material and zero Chapter 13 imports). Following p. 101's inline prose definition (*"a field with only finitely many elements"*), `IsGarlingFiniteField K := setOrder K < ⊤` (`isGarlingFiniteField_iff`) is defined first and `finite_field_char_and_prime_power` deduces `0 < ringChar K`, `Nat.Prime (ringChar K)`, `primeSubfield K ≃+* ZMod (ringChar K)`, `IsFiniteExtension (primeSubfield K) K`, `K ≃ₗ[primeSubfield K] Kn n (primeSubfield K)`, and `|K| = p^n`. `theorem_12_1` (and `theorem_12_1_natCard`) constructs `K` of order `p^n` directly via `Theorem 7.3`, `garlingDeriv (X^{p^n} - X) = -1`, and §10.5's iterated Frobenius fixed subfield `frobeniusIterFixedSubfield`, proves that every field of order `p^n` is a splitting field for `x^{p^n} - x` over `primeSubfield` (`finite_field_isSplittingField_primeSubfield`), and proves uniqueness up to isomorphism across arbitrary universes `u, v` via `Corollary 1` to `Theorem 7.5`. `corollary_1_to_theorem_12_1` (plus `_zmod`) and `corollary_2_to_theorem_12_1` (plus `_subfield` and `_triple`) prove Galoisness over `primeSubfield K` and for any extension `L:K` with `L` finite via `Corollary 1`/`Corollary 3` to `Theorem 9.1`, `Theorem 10.1`, and `Corollary` to `Theorem 10.8`.
64. **`Exercises 12.1, 12.2`, and `Exercise 12.3` (`§12.1`, printed p. 102 — `12.3` `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** `exercise_12_1` (and `exercise_12_1_garling`) holds literally for **every** finite field $K$ of order $q$ (`setOrder K = q`) and **every** prime $p$ (whether or not $p = \operatorname{char} K$), counting the $(q^p - q)/p$ monic irreducible degree-$p$ polynomials by partitioning $L \setminus K$ (`|L| = q^p`, constructed via `exists_extension_card_pow_of_finite_field`) by minimal polynomials of degree $p$ (`Theorem 4.4`). `exercise_12_2` (and `exercise_12_2_triple`) proves existence of an extension $L:K$ of degree $n$ in $K$'s universe together with essential uniqueness (`Nonempty (L₁ ≃ₐ[K] L₂)`) across arbitrary universes `u, v, w`. `Exercise 12.3` omits *"where $p$ is a prime"*: `exercise_12_3_nonprime_counterexample` refutes Part 1 at $p = 4, n = 1, L = \mathbb{F}_4, K = \mathbb{F}_2$ ($|K| = 2 \ne 4^d$), `exercise_12_3` proves both parts under `Nat.Prime p`, and `exercise_12_3_converse_unconditional` proves Part 2 (`(X^{p^d} - X) ∣ (X^{p^n} - X)` and unique subfield of order $p^d$) unconditionally for `setOrder L = (p ^ n : ℕ)` (`Exercise 12.3` is held in `QUEUE.md`).
65. **§12.2 Worked Artin–Schreier example (`pp. 102–103`, printed slip `K` for `ℤ_p`) and `Exercise 12.4` (`p. 103` — `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** Across pp. 102–103, §12.2 writes unbound `K` four times (`L = K(α)`, `[L:K] = p`, and two `L:K` splitting-field clauses) where `ℤ_p` was introduced on p. 102, l. 28; `artinSchreierPoly`, `artinSchreierPoly_irreducible` (following Garling's exact $x^{d-1}$-coefficient argument $-\sum_{\bar{i} \in S}(\alpha + \bar{i})$), and `section_12_2_artin_schreier_example` formalize the full worked example over `ZMod p`. `Exercise 12.4` (*"Factorize $x^{p^p} - x$ over $\mathbb{Z}_p$"*) is held in `QUEUE.md` as a computational prompt (with implicit `Nat.Prime p`): `exercise_12_4` proves the complete monic irreducible factorization `(∏_{a ∈ ℤ_p} (X - C a)) * (∏_{g ∈ monicIrredDegPFinset p} g)` (`p` linear factors + `p^{p-1} - 1 = (p^p - p)/p` degree-$p$ irreducibles, containing all $p - 1$ non-zero Artin–Schreier polynomials), `exercise_12_4_p2` gives `X^4 - X = X (X - 1) (X^2 - X - 1)` over `ℤ_2`, and `exercise_12_4_dossier_formula_false` refutes `DOSSIER-GARLING.md` l. 353's false formula `x ∏_{j=1}^{p-1} (x^p - x - j)` for every prime $p$ (and `(x^p - x) ∏_{j=1}^{p-1} (x^p - x - j)` for every prime $p \ge 3$).
66. **Abelian group structure, exponent `e(G)`, `Σ_3`/`Σ_6` examples, and trivial-group `|G| = 1` generator conventions (`§12.3`, printed pp. 103–105 — `Exercise 12.8` `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** Following p. 103, l. 26 (*"generated by $s$ elements: that is, generated by at most $s$ elements"*), `IsAddGeneratedBy G s` (`Fin s → G`) and §1.1 p. 7's non-empty `IsGarlingAddGeneratedBy G s` (`A.Nonempty`, `isGarlingAddGeneratedBy_iff_of_pos`) are both formalized. For `Theorem 12.2` (pp. 103–104; minor printed subscript slip `j_r g_s` for `j_s g_s` on p. 104, l. 5), **every** natural reading holds for all finite abelian groups: `theorem_12_2` proves invariant factors $1 < d_1 \mid \dots \mid d_s$ (`s = 0` at $|G| = 1$) with both `∀ t < s, ¬ IsAddGeneratedBy G t` (matching p. 103, l. 27) and integer-typed `s - 1 : ℤ`, `theorem_12_2_nontrivial` proves literal `ℕ` subtraction `s - 1` for `[Nontrivial G]`, and `theorem_12_2_garling_nonempty` proves literal `ℕ` subtraction `s - 1` under §1.1's `IsGarlingAddGeneratedBy` for **all** finite abelian groups (including $|G| = 1$ via $s = 1, d_1 = 1$). 67. **Multiplicative group `K*` of a field (`Theorem 12.3` and `Corollaries 1–2`, `§12.4`, printed p. 105):** On p. 105, l. 3 of `Theorem 12.3`'s proof writes *"By Theorem 12.2, $G$ has an element of order $|G|$"* where the `Corollary` to `Theorem 12.2` ($o(g) = e(G)$) is the exact result applied. `theorem_12_3` proves cyclicity of any finite subgroup $G \le K^\times$ of an arbitrary field $K$ directly from `garlingExponent_and_elemOrder_properties`, polynomial root-counting on $X^{e(G)} - 1$, and `corollary_to_theorem_12_2`; `corollary_1_to_theorem_12_3` (`IsCyclic Kˣ` and `Nat.card Kˣ = Nat.card K - 1`) and `corollary_2_to_theorem_12_3` (`_subfield`, `_triple`: `IsSimpleExtension K L` for finite `L`) follow immediately.

Like `Exercise 12.3`, `Theorem 12.4` (p. 105) omits *"where $p$ is a prime"*: `theorem_12_4_nonprime_counterexample` refutes the unguarded claim at $p = 4, n = 1, K = \mathbb{F}_4$ (`Nat.card K = 4^1`, `Nat.card (GarlingAut K) = 2 ≠ 1`), while `theorem_12_4` proves under `Nat.Prime p` that `0 < n`, `ringChar K = p`, `GarlingAut K` is cyclic of order $n$, `orderOf φ = n`, and `Subgroup.zpowers φ = ⊤` (`Theorem 12.4` is held in `QUEUE.md`). `corollary_to_theorem_12_4` (`_subfield`) proves `IsCyclic (GarlingGaloisGroup K L)` and `|Γ(L:K)| = [L:K]` literally for all finite `L`. `Exercise 12.10` (p. 106, held in `QUEUE.md` as a proof-method prompt inheriting `Nat.Prime p`) is proved in `exercise_12_10` **genuinely** via `theorem_12_4`, `corollary_to_theorem_12_4_subfield`, and Chapter 11's FTGT (`theorem_11_8`, `theorem_11_4`) by classifying subgroups of cyclic `⟨φ⟩` of order $n/d$ as uniquely `⟨φ^d⟩` without calling `exercise_12_3`. Finally, `exercise_12_11` (p. 106) proves literally for primes $p < q$ with $p \nmid q - 1$ that $u \mapsto u^p$ is bijective on $(\mathbb{Z}_q)^\times$ and the splitting field $L:\mathbb{Z}_q$ of $x^p - 1$ is simultaneously a splitting field extension for every $x^p - a$ ($a \in \mathbb{Z}_q \setminus \{0\}$).
69. **Literal §14.1 *radical*, *extension by radicals*, and *solvable by radicals* definitions (`n ≥ 1` exponent convention, printed p. 110):** Printed p. 110 says *"$\beta$ is a radical over $K$ if $\beta^n \in K$ for some $n$. Thus a radical over $K$ is an $n$th root of some element of $K$, possibly in a larger field."* `IsGarlingRadicalOver K β` (`∃ n ≥ 1, β ^ n ∈ range (algebraMap K L)`, bridged to subfields in `isGarlingRadicalOver_iff_subfield`) enforces the positive-exponent convention $n \ge 1$ (`1 ≤ n`), without which $\beta^0 = 1 \in K$ would make every element of every extension — including transcendental ones — a radical (`radical_zero_exponent_trivial`), whereas with $n \ge 1$ every radical is algebraic over $K$ (`isGarlingAlgebraic_of_isGarlingRadicalOver`) and every extension by radicals (`IsGarlingExtensionByRadicals K L`, allowing $r = 0$ for $L = K$) is finite (`isFiniteExtension_of_isGarlingExtensionByRadicals`) and closed under towers (`isGarlingExtensionByRadicals_tower`). Following p. 110's remark that in `IsGarlingSolvableByRadicals f` ($f$ splits over *some* radical extension $L:K$ in the same universe `Type u`), $L$ need not be a splitting field for $f$, `exists_radical_step_extension` constructs single-radical extensions via `SplittingField (X^n - C a)` and `quadratic_solvable_by_radicals` solves all quadratics over $\operatorname{char} K \ne 2$.
70. **Leading-coefficient-free *discriminant* `Δ = δ²`, `Theorem 14.1`, Vandermonde/Hankel/quadratic/cubic formulas, and `Exercises 14.1–14.4` (`§14.2`, printed pp. 111–113):** On p. 111, Garling defines $\delta = \prod_{1 \le i < j \le n} (\alpha_j - \alpha_i)$ (`garlingDeltaTuple`) and the *discriminant* $\Delta = \delta^2$ (`garlingDiscTuple`, `garlingDiscriminant`) **without** any $a_n^{2n-2}$ factor, for any $f \in K[x]$ whose roots $\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_n$ are listed with multiplicity in a splitting field $L:K$ (`garlingDiscriminant_eq_discTuple`, descending to $K$ via `garlingDiscTuple_mem_base_of_splittingField`; at $f = 0$, `garlingDiscriminant 0 = 0`, while for $\deg f \le 1$, $\Delta = 1$, `garlingDiscriminant_of_natDegree_le_one`, paired with `garlingDisc_zero_not_unique_of_splitsExplicit` explaining why $f \ne 0$ is needed for multiplicity uniqueness). Consequently:
   - `Theorem 14.1` (p. 111, `theorem_14_1`) holds **literally for all `f : K[X]`** (including $f = 0$, where $\Delta = 0$ and $0$ has repeated roots, and $\deg f \le 1$, where $\Delta = 1 = 1^2 \in K$ makes `(i)` and `(iii)` vacuous and `(ii)` trivial), proving independence of $\Gamma_K(f) \cap A_n$ from root labellings (`garlingEvenGaloisSubgroup_indep`) via `galois_apply_garlingDeltaTuple`.
   - On p. 112, `garlingDeltaTuple_eq_det_vandermonde`, `garlingDiscTuple_eq_det_hankel`, `garlingDiscriminant_monic_quadratic`, and `garlingDiscriminant_monic_cubic` formalize the Vandermonde determinant, Hankel power-sum determinant, quadratic discriminant $a_1^2 - 4a_0$, and monic cubic discriminant $-4a_2^3 a_0 + a_2^2 a_1^2 + 18a_2 a_1 a_0 - 4a_1^3 - 27a_0^2$ (dropped by OCR in `garling1986_pages.txt`).
   - `Exercise 14.1` (p. 112) is held in `QUEUE.md` because p. 112 omits `Monic f`: for general $f = a_n \prod_{j=1}^n (x - \alpha_j)$, $\eta_n \prod_{j=1}^n \mathrm{D}f(\alpha_j) = a_n^n \Delta$ (`exercise_14_1_general`), refuted for non-monic $f = 2x^2 - 2x \in \mathbb{Q}[x]$ (`4 ≠ 1`, `exercise_14_1_nonmonic_counterexample`), while `exercise_14_1` proves equality for all monic $f$ and `exercise_14_1_iff_of_disc_ne_zero` proves exact equivalence with `a_n^n = 1` when $\Delta \ne 0$.
   - `Exercise 14.2` (pp. 112–113, `exercise_14_2_i`, `exercise_14_2_ii`, `exercise_14_2_iii`, `exercise_14_2`) proves `(i)`–`(iii)` (*Newton's identities*) literally for roots listed with multiplicity ($f = a_n \prod_{i=1}^n (x - \alpha_i)$, `n ≥ 1` in `(iii)` since $a_{n-1}$ is indexed), paired with `exercise_14_2_nonmultiplicity_remark` showing `(ii)`–`(iii)` fail on $(x-1)(x+1)$ if one repeats a single root $(1, 1)$.
   - `Exercise 14.3` (p. 113) is held in `QUEUE.md` for two genuine textbook errors: (a) at $n = 2$ ($x^2 + px + q$), the indices $n = 2$ and $2n - 2 = 2$ collide ($\lambda_2 = p^2 - 2q$, refuted on $x^2 + x + 1 \in \mathbb{Q}[x]$ in `exercise_14_3_n2_powerSum_counterexample`), whereas all five power-sum formulas hold for **$n \ge 3$** (`exercise_14_3_powerSums`); and (b) Garling's printed discriminant $\eta_{n+1} n^n q^{n-1} - \eta_n (n - 1)^{n-1} p^n$ swaps $\eta_n \leftrightarrow \eta_{n+1}$ and equals $(-1)^n \Delta$ (`exercise_14_3_printed_eq_neg_one_pow_mul_disc`), negating $\Delta$ for **every odd $n \ge 3$** (refuted at $n = 3$ on $x^3 + 1 \in \mathbb{Q}[x]$ where $\Delta = -27 \ne +27$ in `exercise_14_3_odd_counterexample`), while the true formula $\Delta = \eta_n n^n q^{n-1} - \eta_{n+1} (n - 1)^{n-1} p^n$ holds for all $n \ge 2$ (`exercise_14_3_discriminant`, matching Garling's own Ch. 20 §20.3 application at $n = 5$ on p. 161).
   - `Exercise 14.4` (p. 113, `exercise_14_4`) proves both that every separable $f \in K[x]$ over $\operatorname{char} K = 2$ has $\Delta \in K^2$ (`exercise_14_4_sqrt_in_char_two`, via elementary symmetric expansion of $\delta$ in $\operatorname{char} 2$) and the explicit counterexample $f = x^2 + x + 1 \in \mathbb{Z}_2[x]$ where $\Delta = 1 = 1^2 \in \mathbb{Z}_2$ yet $\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}_2}(f) \cong \Sigma_2 \nsubseteq A_2 = \{e\}$ (`exercise_14_4_counterexample_zmod2`).

27q^2$ (`garlingDiscriminant_depressed_cubic`) alongside an explicit refutation of p. 114 l. 5's printed sign slip `4p^3 - 27q^2` (`section_14_3_printed_disc_sign_typo_remark`), the `Theorem 14.1` reduction $[L:K(\delta)] = 3$ with $\Gamma(L:K(\delta)) \cong A_3$ (`section_14_3_splitting_over_K_delta`), p. 114's 6-field diamond dichotomy $L(\omega) = L$ vs. $[L(\omega):L] = 2$ (`section_14_3_cube_root_dichotomy`), the resolvent identities $\beta\gamma = -3p$, $\beta^3 + \gamma^3 = -27q$, $(x - For the §14.4 exercises:

81. **Kummer extensions (`Theorems 16.5–16.6` and `Exercises 16.15–16.16`, `§16.4`, printed pp. 128–130):** `has_d_distinct_roots_X_pow_sub_one_iff` bridges `(X^d - 1 : K[X]).roots.toFinset.card = d` to `¬ (ringChar K ∣ d) ∧ ∃ ω : K, IsPrimitiveRoot ω d` for all $1 \le d$. `Theorem 16.5` (`theorem_16_5`) is proved literally as printed (decomposing abelian $\Gamma(L:K)$ via Chapter 12's `theorem_12_2` and applying `Theorem 16.4` to each cyclic quotient $M_j = L^{\ker \pi_j}$, yielding $1 \le r$ even when $\Gamma(L:K) = 1$; note `garling1986_pages.txt` deletes the displayed $\beta_r$ formula on p. 129). `IsGarlingKummerExtension K L d` formalizes p. 129's prose definition (minor typos `"As extension"` and `"θ_j"` for `"θ_r"`) with explicit `1 ≤ d` to avoid Mathlib's junk `(X^0 - 1 : K[X]).roots = 0` (`theorem_16_6_zero_exponent_mathlib_trap`), holding `Theorem 16.6` (`theorem_16_6`, proved via deduplicating nonzero $\theta_j$ for Galoisness + Chapter 11 `theorem_11_6` for abelianness and exponent divisibility) in `QUEUE.md`. `Exercise 16.15` (`exercise_16_15`) is proved literally for all $n \in \mathbb{N}$ (deducing $1 \le n$ from irreducibility, handling $n = 1$ directly and $n \ge 2$ by inverting $r$ in `(ZMod n)ˣ`). `Exercise 16.16` is held in `QUEUE.md` for the `n = 1, a = 0, b = 1` boundary sensitivity between positive `r ≥ 1` (`exercise_16_16_n1_pos_r_counterexample`) and `r : ℕ` allowing `r = 0` (`exercise_16_16` unconditionally for all $n$, plus `exercise_16_16_pos_r` giving $1 \le r < n$ for all $2 \le n$).
82. **Garling's cyclic-factor *soluble* definition (`IsGarlingSoluble`), *simple* groups (`IsGarlingSimple`), and §17.1 prose examples/theorems (`§17.1`, printed pp. 131–132):** Garling (§17.1, p. 131) defines a group $G$ to be *soluble* (`IsGarlingSoluble G`) if there exists a finite subnormal series $\{e\} = G_n \trianglelefteq G_{n-1} \trianglelefteq \dots \trianglelefteq G_0 = G$ with each quotient $G_i / G_{i+1}$ **cyclic** (the definition of a *polycyclic* group on infinite groups, strictly stronger than Mathlib's `IsSolvable G` with abelian factors!). `Garling/Chapter17.lean` formalizes `IsGarlingSoluble` literally as printed, proves `isSolvable_of_isGarlingSoluble`, `group_fg_of_isGarlingSoluble`, and `additive_rat_isSolvable_not_isGarlingSoluble` (`Multiplicative ℚ` is abelian/Mathlib-solvable but not finitely generated and therefore **not** `IsGarlingSoluble`), and proves `isGarlingSoluble_iff_isSolvable_of_finite` on every finite group. Similarly, `IsGarlingSimple G` formalizes p. 132's prose definition (*"no normal subgroups other than $\{e\}$ and $G$"*, bridged via `isSimpleGroup_iff_nontrivial_and_isGarlingSimple`). For §17.1's prose examples and unnumbered prose theorem: `section_17_1_soluble_examples` proves solubility of $A_3, \Sigma_3, A_4, \Sigma_4$ (correcting p. 131's harmless prose slip `G_3 = {e, (12)}` in $\Sigma_4$ to a subgroup of $N = \text{viergruppe}$, e.g. `⟨(12)(34)⟩`), every finite abelian group (`isGarlingSoluble_of_commGroup_finite`), and every finitely generated abelian group (`isGarlingSoluble_of_commGroup_fg`); `section_17_1_p131_le_five_typo_witness` records that p. 131 l. 30's printed `n ≤ 5` (masked as `n ~ 5` in OCR) is a typo for `n ≥ 5` by refuting simplicity of $A_4$ (`¬ IsGarlingSimple (alternatingGroup (Fin 4)) ∧ ¬ IsSimpleGroup (alternatingGroup (Fin 4))`) via `viergruppe`; and `section_17_1_alternating_simple_and_not_soluble` proves that for every $n \ge 5$, $A_n$ is simple (`IsSimpleGroup` and `IsGarlingSimple`) and not soluble (`¬ IsGarlingSoluble` and `¬ IsSolvable`).

95. **Genuine characteristic-$2$/characteristic-$3$ textbook error in `Theorem 19.3` Clause 4 (`§19.1`, printed pp. 148–149 — `QUEUE.md` fidelity hold):** Garling prints *"The generic polynomial $x^n - a_1 x^{n-1} + \dots + (-1)^n a_n$ is irreducible over $K(a_1, \dots, a_n)$. It is separable, and its Galois group is isomorphic to $\Sigma_n$. It is solvable by radicals if and only if $n \leqslant 4$."* without any characteristic guard (and without $n \ge 1$ for irreducibility). Because §14.1 (`IsGarlingSolvableByRadicals`) imposes no characteristic restriction, Clause 4's `⇐` direction is **mathematically false** whenever $\operatorname{char} K \mid n!$ and $n \le 4$ — i.e., in characteristic $2$ for $n \in \{2, 3, 4\}$ and in characteristic $3$ for $n \in \{3, 4\}$. Passing to an algebraic closure $\overline{K}$ (where $a_1, \dots, a_n$ remain algebraically independent, `isGarlingAlgIndepTuple_over_algebraic`), reducing every $p^{e_i} q_i$-th radical step by iterated Frobenius $\eta_i = \beta_i^{p^{D_i}}$ into a radical tower of exponent $q_i$ with $p \nmid q_i$ (`frobenius_reduction_of_radical_extension`), and applying `Theorem 16.1` step-by-step over `Kbar` (`not_char_dvd_finrank_of_radical_over_algClosed`, `not_char_dvd_factorial_of_generic_splits_in_algClosed`) alongside `Theorem 17.6` / `Theorem 17.3` in `theorem_19_3_solvable_iff`, `Garling/Chapter19.lean` kernel-proves: (a) `theorem_19_3_clauses_1_to_3` (irreducibility, separability, and $\Gamma_f \cong \Sigma_n$ for all `1 ≤ n` and arbitrary characteristic), (b) `theorem_19_3_n0_not_irreducible` ($n = 0$ unit counterexample), (c) `theorem_19_3_solvable_iff` (**universal exact characterization** across **all** fields $K$ and **all** `n : ℕ`: `IsGarlingSolvableByRadicals f ↔ n ≤ 4 ∧ ¬ (ringChar K ∣ n.factorial)`), (d) `theorem_19_3_char_dvd_factorial_not_solvable` (all `ringChar K ∣ n!`, including `char K = 2, n ∈ {2, 3, 4}` and `char K = 3, n ∈ {3, 4}`), `theorem_19_3_char_two_counterexample` (`K = ZMod 2`, `n = 2`), and `theorem_19_3_char_three_counterexample` (`K = ZMod 3`, `n = 3`), (e) `theorem_19_3_repaired`, and (f) `theorem_19_3_of_char_zero_or_ge_five` (confirming the literal `↔ n ≤ 4` equivalence holds whenever `ringChar K = 0 ∨ 5 ≤ ringChar K`).
96. **Symmetric polynomials, tower $M_j$ / `Exercise 19.1`, `Exercise 19.2`, and `Theorem 19.4` + `Corollary` (`§19.1`, printed pp. 149–150):** `IsGarlingSymmetricPoly` (`∀ σ : SigmaN n, MvPolynomial.rename σ f = f`, bridged to `permAut` via `isGarlingSymmetricPoly_iff_permAut`) and `elemSymmSubst` ($g \mapsto g(s_1, \dots, s_n)$) anchor the second half of §19.1. `towerField`, `towerStabSubgroup`, `towerPrefixPoly`, `towerSuffixPoly`, and `towerSuffixPolyOver` prove `exercise_19_1` literally for all $0 \le j \le n$ ($M_j = \phi(G_j)$ and $[E:M_j] = (n - j)!$), `section_19_1_tower_step` ($[M_{j+1}:M_j] = n - j$, minimal polynomial $g_j$, irreducibility, and power-basis independence), `section_19_1_p149_strict_bound_typo_remark` (documenting p. 149 l. 26's off-by-one slip `0 ≤ i_k < n - k` vs. the true `0 ≤ i_k ≤ n - k` of cardinality `n!`), `section_19_1_multi_index_linearIndependent`, and `section_19_1_multi_index_basis` (proving the `n!` multi-index monomials form a `K(s₁, …, sₙ)`-basis of `E`). In `Exercise 19.2`, `lexFollows` (proved strict total order and well-founded in `lexFollows_wf`) and `HasPrincipalTerm` (requiring non-zero support term `a ≠ 0`, hence `f ≠ 0`) prove all four parts `(i)`–`(iv)` literally in `exercise_19_2_i`, `exercise_19_2_ii`, `exercise_19_2_iii_domain`, `elemSymmSubst_injective`, and `exercise_19_2`, yielding `theorem_19_4_domain` (arbitrary integral domain $R$), `theorem_19_4` (field $K$), `section_19_1_int_symmetric_remark` ($\mathbb{Z}$-integrality remark below `Theorem 19.4`), and `corollary_to_theorem_19_4` ($f(\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_n) = g(a_1, \dots, a_n)$ for $h = \prod (x - \alpha_i)$ counting multiplicity, noting OCR's corruption of $\alpha_i$ to $a_i$).
97. **Non-vanishing on infinite grids (`Theorem 19.5`), *normal basis* (`Theorem 19.6`), and cyclotomic normal bases (`Exercise 19.3`) (`§19.2`, printed pp. 150–152):** `theorem_19_5` is proved literally for every integral domain $S$, infinite subset $R \subseteq S$, `n : ℕ`, and non-zero $f \in S[x_1, \dots, x_n]$ by induction on $n$ via `MvPolynomial.finSuccEquiv` and `MvPolynomial.eval_eq_eval_mv_eval'`. Since §19.2's main text never defines the term *"normal basis"* before `Exercise 19.3` (p. 152), `IsGarlingNormalBasisGenerator` and `IsGarlingNormalBasisSet` formalize both the orbit-generator and basis-subset forms; `theorem_19_6` proves the normal basis theorem literally for every infinite field $K$, Galois extension $L:K$, and enumeration $\sigma : \operatorname{Fin} n \simeq \Gamma(L:K)$ following Garling's pp. 151–152 proof via `Theorem 19.5` and `Chapter11.exercise_11_3` (itself proved from `Theorem 11.2`). Finally, `exercise_19_3` holds **literally for every `n : ℕ`** under Chapter 15's `IsGarlingPrimitiveRoot` (at $n = 0$, `{ε | IsGarlingPrimitiveRoot 0 ε} = ∅` is not a basis and `¬ Squarefree 0`; at $n = 1$, `{1}` is a normal basis of $\mathbb{Q}:\mathbb{Q}$ and `Squarefree 1`; for $n \ge 2$, `⇐` inducts on prime factors of squarefree $n$ inside the ambient field $L$ via `span_primitiveRoots_eq_top_of_squarefree` and `⇒` builds the linear dependence $\sum_{j=0}^{p-1} \eta^j \varepsilon_0 = 0$ whenever $p^2 \mid n$), paired with the `1 ≤ n` Mathlib `IsPrimitiveRoot` companion.
98. **Regular polygons (`IsPossible`), `Theorems 19.7–19.9`, and *Fermat primes* (`§19.3`, printed pp. 152–154; `Theorems 19.7` and `19.9` in `QUEUE.md`):** Following p. 152's inline prose definition (*"given an integer $n > 2$, can we construct a regular polygon with $n$ sides of unit length, using ruler and compasses alone? Let us say that an integer $n$ is possible if this can be done"*), `IsPossible n := 2 < n ∧ IsUnitSideRegularPolygonConstructible n` explicitly carries `2 < n` (bridged to `(x_n, y_n) = (cos(2π/n), sin(2π/n))` and all four Chapter 6 formulations via `isPossible_iff_constructible_point`, with `isPossible_n1_n2_false` recording that `¬ IsPossible 1 ∧ ¬ IsPossible 2` even though `(x_1, y_1) = (1, 0)` and `(x_2, y_2) = (-1, 0)` are constructible). All p. 152 prose reductions (`IsConstructibleAngle`, addition/subtraction/`|θ - φ|`/bisection, `isPossible_two_mul`, `isPossible_pow_two`, `isPossible_mul_of_coprime`, `isPossible_of_dvd`, and `section_19_3_small_examples` for `3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10` vs. `7, 9`) are kernel-proved.
    - `Theorem 19.7` (p. 152) and `Theorem 19.9 (Gauss)` (p. 154) are held in `QUEUE.md` because both omit §19.3's standing `2 < n` hypothesis while allowing $r = 0$ (required for $n = 4, 8, \dots$), so $n = 1$ ($t = 0, r = 0$) and $n = 2$ ($t = 1, r = 0$) satisfy both RHS formulas while `¬ IsPossible 1` and `¬ IsPossible 2` (`theorem_19_7_n1_n2_counterexamples`, `theorem_19_9_n1_n2_counterexamples`); `theorem_19_7_repaired`, `theorem_19_7_iff`, `theorem_19_9_repaired` (both `Finset` and injective `Fin r` forms), and `theorem_19_9_iff` prove all repaired and unconditional characterizations.
    - `Theorem 19.8` (p. 153, `theorem_19_8`) holds **literally for every odd prime `p` and every `b : ℕ`** (including $b = 0$, where $p^0 = 1$ is not possible by `2 < n`): `⇒` applies `Chapter06.theorem_6_1`, adjoins `i` (`[ℚ(x_n, y_n, i) : ℚ] = 2^{r+1}`), and uses `Chapter15.theorem_15_3` to get $p^{b-1}(p - 1) \mid 2^{r+1}$; `⇐` uses `Chapter15.corollary_to_theorem_15_4` and `Chapter15.theorem_15_4` on the splitting field of $\Phi_p$, builds the descending cyclic subgroup chain $H_j = \langle \sigma^{2^j} \rangle$ (correcting p. 153 l. 26's reversed subgroup-indexing slip) and ascending fixed-field quadratic tower in $\mathbb{C}$, and applies `section_19_3_complex_quadratic_tower_constructible` (completing the square algebraically via `section_19_3_complex_quadratic_step_constructible`).
    - `IsFermatPrime p := Nat.Prime p ∧ ∃ k : ℕ, 1 ≤ k ∧ p = 2 ^ k + 1` builds in `1 ≤ k` to exclude $p = 2 = 2^0 + 1$ (`section_19_3_fermat_prime_k0_remark`, matching p. 154 l. 1's $k = 2^l$ claim and the list starting at $3$); `section_19_3_fermat_prime_exponent_pow_two`, `isFermatPrime_iff_pow_two_pow`, `isFermatPrime_iff_odd_prime`, `section_19_3_known_fermat_primes` (`3, 5, 17, 257, 65537` proved purely in-kernel via `norm_num`, zero `native_decide`), and `section_19_3_p154_k_for_l_typo_remark` (documenting p. 154 l. 5's `k` for `l` slip) are all kernel-proved.
